\mathbb{R}
and
\frac{1}{\delta}
-Lipschitz, by the properties
(P4)
and
(P6)
, we infer that
F_{\delta}
satisfies the linear growth condition, i.e. there exists a positive constant
\tilde{c}_{\delta}
depending on
\delta,\,\tilde{c}_{F},\,F(0)
such that

\lvert F_{\delta}(s)\rvert\leq\tilde{c}_{\delta}(1+\lvert s\rvert^{2}),\;\;s\in\mathbb{R}.

(4.3)

In a similar way, we define the Yosida approximation
G_{\delta}
of
G
.

We fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and we consider the following regularized stochastic Cahn-Hilliard-Brinkman problem

\displaystyle-\diver(2\nu(\phi_{\delta})D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta})+\lambda(\phi_{\delta})\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta}+\nabla p_{\delta}=\mu_{\delta}\nabla\phi_{\delta}\quad

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }Q_{T},

(4.4a)

\displaystyle\diver\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta}=0

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }Q_{T},

(4.4b)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\phi_{\delta}+[\diver(\phi_{\delta}\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta})-\diver(M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta})\nabla\mu_{\delta})]\mathrm{d}t=F_{1}(\phi_{\delta})\mathrm{d}W

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }Q_{T},

(4.4c)

\displaystyle\mu_{\delta}=-\varepsilon\Delta\phi_{\delta}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }Q_{T},

(4.4d)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\varphi_{\delta}+[\diver_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta}\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta})-\diver_{\Gamma}(M_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta})\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta})]\mathrm{d}t=F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\mbox{ on }\Sigma_{T},

(4.5a)

\displaystyle\theta_{\delta}=-\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\Delta_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta})+\varepsilon\partial_{\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}}\phi_{\delta}

\displaystyle\mbox{ on }\Sigma_{T},

(4.5b)

\displaystyle K\partial_{\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}}\phi_{\delta}=\varphi_{\delta}-\phi_{\delta}

\displaystyle\mbox{ on }\Sigma_{T},

(4.5c)

together with the following boundary and initial conditions

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta})\partial_{\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}}\mu_{\delta}=\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}=0

\displaystyle\mbox{ on }\Sigma_{T},

(4.6a)

\displaystyle[2\nu(\phi_{\delta})(D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta})\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}+\gamma(\varphi_{\delta})\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta}]_{\tau}=-[\varphi_{\delta}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta}]_{\tau}

\displaystyle\mbox{ on }\Sigma_{T},

(4.6b)

\displaystyle\phi_{\delta}(0)=\phi_{0}\mbox{ in }\mathcal{O}\mbox{ and }\varphi_{\delta}(0)=\varphi_{0}

\displaystyle\mbox{ on }\Gamma.

(4.7a)

Next, we proved the existence of a martingale solution to Problem (
4.4
)-(
4.7a
) in the sense of Definition (
3.9
), but with the potential
F
being replaced by its Yosida approximation
F_{\delta}
.

4.2.
Galerkin approximation

Since both
\mathcal{O}
and
\Gamma
are bounded, the classical spectral Theorem and the properties of our three linear operators imply the following assertions.

There exists an increasing sequence
(\xi_{j})_{j=1}^{\infty}
with
\xi_{1}>0
and
\xi_{j}\nearrow\infty
as
j\to\infty
, the eigenvalues of the Stokes operator
A
, and a family
\{\mbox{\boldmath{$\mathrm{w}$}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}\subset D(\mbox{\boldmath{$A$}})
of eigen-functions of
A
that is orthonormal in
H
and such that
\mbox{\boldmath{$A$}}\mbox{\boldmath{$\mathrm{w}$}}_{j}=\xi_{j}\mbox{\boldmath{$\mathrm{w}$}}_{j}
,
j=1,2,\ldots
.

The Laplace-Beltrami operator
\Delta_{\Gamma}
admits a spectral decomposition, i.e. there exist an orthonormal
basis
\{\bar{\Lambda}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset H^{2}(\Gamma)
of
L^{2}(\Gamma)
, consisting of eigenvectors of
\Delta_{\Gamma}
and a set of eigenvalues
\{\lambda^{\Gamma}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{N}
, where

-\Delta_{\Gamma}\bar{\Lambda}_{i}=\lambda^{\Gamma}_{i}\bar{\Lambda}_{i}\mbox{ on }\Gamma.

(4.8)

There exists an orthonormal basis
\{\bar{\upsilon}_{j}\in H^{2}(\mathcal{O}):j=1,2,\ldots\}
of
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
,
whose elements are the eigen-functions of the Poisson-Neumann eigenvalue problem

\begin{cases}-\Delta\bar{\upsilon}_{j}&=\lambda^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\bar{\upsilon}_{j}\mbox{ in }\mathcal{O},\\
\frac{\partial\bar{\upsilon}_{j}}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}}&=0\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{ on }\Gamma,\end{cases}

(4.9)

where
\lambda^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
are the corresponding eigenvalues.
We assume that
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{F},\mathbb{P})
is a filtered probability space satisfying the usual assumptions, cf. Section
3
. We also fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
T>0
.

Define the following finite-dimensional linear subspaces,

\displaystyle V^{n}

\displaystyle\coloneq\mathrm{linspan}\{\mbox{\boldmath{$\mathrm{w}$}}_{1},\ldots,\mbox{\boldmath{$\mathrm{w}$}}_{n}\}\subset V,\quad H_{n}^{1}\coloneq\mathrm{linspan}\{\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\upsilon}_{n}\}\subset H^{1}(\mathcal{O}),

\displaystyle H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}

\displaystyle\coloneq\mathrm{linspan}\{\bar{\Lambda}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\Lambda}_{n}\}\subset H^{1}(\Gamma),\quad\mathcal{V}_{n}\coloneq\mathrm{linspan}\{(\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\Lambda}_{1}),\ldots,(\bar{\upsilon}_{n},\bar{\Lambda}_{n})\}\subset\mathbb{V}.

Next, we denote by
P_{n}
the
H
-orthogonal projection onto
V^{n}
and by
\mathcal{S}_{n}
the orthogonal projection from
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
onto
H_{n}^{1}
.
We also denote by
\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}
the orthogonal projection from
L^{2}(\Gamma)
onto
H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}
.

Now, we seek an approximation to a solution of problem (
4.4a
)-(
4.7a
) and the initial condition
(\phi_{0,n},\varphi_{0,n})
of the following forms:

\displaystyle\phi_{\delta,n}(t,\cdot,\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}a_{k}^{n}(t,\omega)\bar{\upsilon}_{k}(\cdot)\in H_{n}^{1},\quad\varphi_{\delta,n}(t,\cdot,\omega)=\sum_{k=1}^{n}b_{k}^{n}(t,\omega)\bar{\Lambda}_{k}(\cdot)\in H_{\Gamma,n}^{1},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mu_{\delta,n}(t,\cdot,\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}c_{k}^{n}(t,\omega)\bar{\upsilon}_{k}(\cdot)\in H_{n}^{1},\quad\theta_{\delta,n}(t,\cdot,\omega)=\sum_{k=1}^{n}d_{k}^{n}(t,\omega)\bar{\Lambda}_{k}(\cdot)\in H_{\Gamma,n}^{1},

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(t,\cdot,\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}e_{k}^{n}(t,\omega)\mbox{\boldmath{$\mathrm{w}$}}_{k}(\cdot)\in V^{n},\;\;(t,\omega)\in(0,T)\times\Omega,

\phi_{\delta,n}(0)=\mathcal{S}_{n}\phi_{\delta}(0)=\mathcal{S}_{n}\phi_{0}\coloneq\phi_{0,n},~\varphi_{\delta,n}(0)=\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta}(0)=\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\varphi_{0}\coloneq\varphi_{0,n}.

Let us fix
t\in[0,T]
. We introduce the bilinear form
a_{n,t}
defined by

\displaystyle a_{n,t}:V\times V\ni(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}},\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}})\mapsto\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left[2\nu(\phi_{\delta,n}(t))D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}:\nabla\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}+\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n}(t))\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right]+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\varphi_{\delta,n}(t))\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}S\in\mathbb{R},

which is part of the weak formulation of the Brinkman equation with the Navier-slip boundary condition. Note that
a_{n,t}
is
V
-continuous and
V
-coercive, i.e. there exist positive constants
C_{0}
and
C_{5}
such that for all
\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\in V
,

a_{n,t}(\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}},\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}})\geq C_{0}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\|_{V}^{2}\mbox{ and }\lvert a_{n,t}(\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}},\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}})\rvert\leq C_{5}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\|_{V}^{2}.

(4.11)

Indeed, if
\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\in V
, then by the trace theorem, there exist a bounded linear operator
\mathcal{T}_{\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}}:\mathbb{H}^{1}(\mathcal{O})\to\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma)
satisfying
\mathcal{T}_{\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}}\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}=\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\lvert_{\Gamma}
and a positive constant
C
depending on
\mathcal{O}
such that

\|\mathcal{T}_{\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}}\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\mathcal{O})}
.
This, jointly with the assumptions
(H4)
-
(H6)
, implies that,

\displaystyle\lvert a_{n,t}(\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}},\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}})\rvert=\left\lvert 2\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nu(\phi_{\delta,n}(t))\lvert D\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathcal{O}}\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n}(t))\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\varphi_{\delta,n}(t))\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{\nu}_{0}\int_{\mathcal{O}}D\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}:D\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\lambda_{0}\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}+\bar{\gamma}_{0}\|\mathcal{T}_{\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}}\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq\bar{\nu}_{0}\lvert\nabla\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}+\lambda_{0}\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}+\bar{\gamma}_{0}C\|\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}.

From this latter inequality, we deduce that for every
t>0
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\lvert a_{n,t}(\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}},\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}})\rvert\leq\bar{\nu}_{0}\lvert\nabla\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}+\lambda_{0}\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}+\bar{\gamma}_{0}C\|\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}\leq C(\bar{\nu}_{0},\lambda_{0},\bar{\gamma}_{0},\mathcal{O})\|\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\|_{V}^{2},\;\;\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\in V.

This proves the second part of (
4.11
).

Next, using
(H4)
-
(H6)
together with the Korn inequality (
2.3
), we infer that

\displaystyle a_{n,t}(\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}},\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}})

\displaystyle\geq 2\nu_{0}\int_{\mathcal{O}}D\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}:D\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\gamma_{0}\int_{\Gamma}\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\geq\min(2\nu_{0},\gamma_{0})\left[\int_{\mathcal{O}}D\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}:D\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\right]

\displaystyle\geq C(\nu_{0},\gamma_{0},C_{\text{KN}})\lvert\nabla\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\rvert^{2},\;\;\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\in V.

This proves the first part of (
4.11
).
From now on, since
a_{n,t}
is
V
-continuous and
V
-coercive, using the Riesz lemma and Lax-Milgram theorem, see, for instance,
[
32
, Theorem 1.6]
, we deduce that there exists a unique
V^{n}
-valued process
\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(t)
solving
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. the following equation,

a_{n,t}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(t),\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}})=-\int_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}(t)\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}(t)\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}S-\int_{\mathcal{O}}\phi_{\delta,n}(t)\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}(t)\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x,\;\;\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\in V^{n}\subset V.

(4.12)

Next, we require that the sequence of processes
(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n},\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
solves the following finite dimensional problem for all
\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\in V^{n}
,
\upsilon\in H_{n}^{1}
,
\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\in H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}
, and
(\psi,\psi\lvert_{\Gamma})\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
,

\displaystyle 2\int_{Q_{t}}\nu(\phi_{\delta,n})D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}:D\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{Q_{t}}\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n})\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\gamma(\varphi_{\delta,n})\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\varphi_{\delta,n}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s-\int_{Q_{t}}\phi_{\delta,n}\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s,

(4.13)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\hskip 10.3473pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\phi_{\delta,n}(t),\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-46.31424pt\hskip 46.31424pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-10.3473pt\hskip-46.31424ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 46.31424ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 10.3473pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\phi_{\delta,n}(t),\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-46.31424pt\hskip 46.31424pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-10.3473pt\hskip-46.31424ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 46.31424ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 9.4307pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\phi_{\delta,n}(t),\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-38.74026pt\hskip 38.74026pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-9.4307pt\hskip-38.74026ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 38.74026ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 9.4307pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\phi_{\delta,n}(t),\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-36.38226pt\hskip 36.38226pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-9.4307pt\hskip-36.38226ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 36.38226ptV_{1}}}}-\int_{Q_{t}}\phi_{\delta,n}\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla\upsilon\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{Q_{t}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla\upsilon\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.14)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{\hskip 10.3473pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\phi_{0,n},\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.27672pt\hskip 35.27672pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-10.3473pt\hskip-35.27672ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 35.27672ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 10.3473pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\phi_{0,n},\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-35.27672pt\hskip 35.27672pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-10.3473pt\hskip-35.27672ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 35.27672ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 9.4307pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\phi_{0,n},\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-29.60136pt\hskip 29.60136pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-9.4307pt\hskip-29.60136ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 29.60136ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 9.4307pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\phi_{0,n},\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-28.2792pt\hskip 28.2792pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-9.4307pt\hskip-28.2792ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 28.2792ptV_{1}}}}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W,\upsilon\right),

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\hskip 11.0973pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\varphi_{\delta,n}(t),\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-55.11981pt\hskip 55.11981pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-11.0973pt\hskip-55.11981ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 55.11981ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 11.0973pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\varphi_{\delta,n}(t),\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-55.11981pt\hskip 55.11981pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-11.0973pt\hskip-55.11981ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 55.11981ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 10.1807pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\varphi_{\delta,n}(t),\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-46.6223pt\hskip 46.6223pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-10.1807pt\hskip-46.6223ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 46.6223ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 10.1807pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\varphi_{\delta,n}(t),\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.19482pt\hskip 44.19482pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-10.1807pt\hskip-44.19482ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 44.19482ptV_{\Gamma}}}}+\int_{\Sigma_{t}}(-\varphi_{\delta,n}\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}+M_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma})\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.15)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{\hskip 11.0973pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\varphi_{0,n},\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.08229pt\hskip 44.08229pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-11.0973pt\hskip-44.08229ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 44.08229ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 11.0973pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\varphi_{0,n},\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-44.08229pt\hskip 44.08229pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-11.0973pt\hskip-44.08229ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 44.08229ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 10.1807pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\varphi_{0,n},\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-37.4834pt\hskip 37.4834pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-10.1807pt\hskip-37.4834ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 37.4834ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 10.1807pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\varphi_{0,n},\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-36.09177pt\hskip 36.09177pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-10.1807pt\hskip-36.09177ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 36.09177ptV_{\Gamma}}}}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma},\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\right)_{\Gamma},

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{t}}\mu_{\delta,n}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\theta_{\delta,n}\psi\lvert_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s-\varepsilon\int_{Q_{t}}\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla\psi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s-\int_{Q_{t}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})\psi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\lvert_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\psi\lvert_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.16)

\displaystyle\qquad+(1/K)\int_{\Sigma_{t}}(\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n})(\varepsilon\psi\lvert_{\Gamma}-\varepsilon\psi)\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s,\;\;t\in[0,T].

Throughout, we set

\displaystyle B_{1,n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}},\phi)

\displaystyle\coloneq\mathcal{S}_{n}B_{1}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}},\phi),\quad\tilde{B}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}},\varphi)\coloneq\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\tilde{B}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}},\varphi),

(4.17)

\displaystyle F_{1,n}(\phi)

\displaystyle\coloneq\left\{U\ni y\mapsto\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{S}_{n}\sigma_{k}(\phi)y_{k}\in H_{n}^{1}\right\}\in\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,H_{n}^{1}),

\displaystyle F_{2,n}(\varphi)

\displaystyle\coloneq\left\{U_{\Gamma}\ni y\mapsto\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\tilde{\sigma}_{k}(\varphi)y_{k}\in H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}\right\}\in\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}),

and we note that

\displaystyle[F_{1,n}(\phi)](e_{1,k})=\mathcal{S}_{n}\sigma_{k}(\phi),\;\;\phi\in H_{n}^{1},\mbox{ and }[F_{2,n}(\varphi)](e_{2,k})=\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\tilde{\sigma}_{k}(\varphi),\;\;\varphi\in H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}.

From now on, (
4.2
)-(
4.16
), can be seen as an ordinary stochastic differential equations in finite-dimensional space
H_{n}^{1}\times H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}
and the solution is an
H_{n}^{1}\times H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}
-valued process
(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})
such that

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\phi_{\delta,n}=[-B_{1,n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})-\mathcal{S}_{n}A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n})]\,\mathrm{d}t+F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W(t),\\
\mathrm{d}\varphi_{\delta,n}=[-\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi_{\delta,n}}(\theta_{\delta,n})-\tilde{B}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})]\,\mathrm{d}t+F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma},\\
\mu_{\delta,n}(t)=\mathcal{S}_{n}[-\varepsilon\Delta\phi_{\delta,n}(t)+\varepsilon^{-1}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n}(t))],\;\;t\in[0,T],\\
\theta_{\delta,n}(t)=\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}[-\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\Delta_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}(t)+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}^{-1}G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n}(t))+\varepsilon\partial_{\mbox{\boldmath{$n$}}}\phi_{\delta,n}(t)],\;\;t\in[0,T],\\
(\phi_{\delta,n}(0,\cdot),\varphi_{\delta,n}(0,\cdot))=(\phi_{0,n},\varphi_{0,n}).\end{cases}

(4.18)

The drift in that system is a function

\displaystyle\mathbf{b}_{n}:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{n}\ni\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\coloneq(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\mapsto\Bigl(-B_{1,n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})-\mathcal{S}_{n}A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n}),

(4.19)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi_{\delta,n}}(\theta_{\delta,n})-\tilde{B}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\Bigr)\in\mathcal{V}_{n},

with
\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}:[0,T]\times\Omega\to V^{n}
being the process obtained previously through an application of the Riesz lemma and Lax-Milgram theorem. Note that
\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}
satisfies the equation (
4.12
).

The diffusion coefficient is

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{n}:\mathcal{V}_{n}\ni\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}=(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\mapsto\mathrm{diag}\left(F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n}),F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\right)\in\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V}_{n}).

(4.20)

Using the notation introduced above, the system (
4.18
) can be written in the following compact form:

\mathrm{d}\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}=\mathbf{b}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}t+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{W}.

(4.21)

Before we continuous, let us think a bit about the above equation.
First of all, the space
\mathcal{V}_{n}
is finite-dimensional and, therefore, all norms on it are equivalent. In the calculations below, we will use the norm which is the norm inherited from the bulk surface product space
\mathbb{V}
. Thus, we will use the Itô Lemma to the free energy functional

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{V}_{n}\ni(\phi,\varphi)\mapsto\Bigl[\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}F_{\delta}(\phi)\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}{2}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi\rvert^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}G_{\delta}(\varphi)

(4.22)

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2K}(\varphi-\phi)^{2}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}S\Bigr]\in\mathbb{R}.

Let us point out that had if the maps
\boldsymbol{b}_{n}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{n}
were globally Lipschitz, one would be able to apply the standard theory of an stochastic differential equations in finite dimensional spaces. However, this is not the case. Fortunately, by some clever calculations, we can prove that
the system (
4.21
) satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
3.3
. Hence,
we deduce that there exists a global maximal solution, i.e. an adapted process
\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}=\{(\phi_{\delta,n}(t),\varphi_{\delta,n}(t)),t\in[0,T]\}
, in
\mathcal{V}_{n}
, unique solution of the stochastic differential equation (
4.21
). In particular, we infer that for every
r\geq 2
,

\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\in L^{r}(\Omega;\,C([0,T];\mathcal{V}_{n}))\mbox{ and }(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\in L^{r}(\Omega;\,C([0,T];\mathcal{V}_{n})).

4.3.
Uniform estimates

Here, we will establish the uniform energy estimate for the solution of the finite-dimensional stochastic differential equation (
4.21
). For this, we define a sequence of stopping times
\{\tau_{\kappa}\}_{\kappa\in\mathbb{N}}
,

\tau_{\kappa}\coloneq\inf\{t>0:(\|\phi_{\delta,n}(t)\|_{V_{1}}^{2}+\|\varphi_{\delta,n}(t)-\phi_{\delta,n}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}(t)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\kappa\}\wedge T

(4.23)

and the following functional

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{tot}:\mathcal{V}_{n}\ni(\phi,\varphi)\mapsto\left[\mathcal{E}(\phi,\varphi)+\frac{1}{2}\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}\right]\in\mathbb{R}.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
be fixed. Assume the assumptions of Theorem
3.11
hold. Then, the sequence
(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n},\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}),\varphi_{\delta,n}(t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}))+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}[2\nu(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}]\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.24)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}M_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{n}(0),\varphi_{n}(0))+\frac{\varepsilon}{2K}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}(\|F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|\nabla F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{2}(U,\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|\nabla_{\Gamma}F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}(\mu_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W)+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}(\theta_{\delta,n},(F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n}))\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\frac{1}{2\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\int_{\Gamma}G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\varepsilon(1/K)\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}(F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k})(F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k})\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}(\phi_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W).

Proof.

By testing (
4.2
) by
\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}
and (
4.15
) by
\theta_{\delta,n}
, respectively, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}[2\nu(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.25)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s-\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\phi_{\delta,n}\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s,\;t\in[0,T].

Next, we apply the Itô formula to the free energy functional
\mathcal{E}
introduced in (
4.22
).

The first Fréchet derivative
D\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{V}_{n}\to\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{E}
is given by

\displaystyle D\mathcal{E}(\phi,\varphi)[(v,v_{1})]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla v\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathcal{O}}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi)v\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}v_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\int_{\Gamma}G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi)v_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S+\varepsilon(1/K)\int_{\Gamma}(\varphi-\phi)(v_{1}-v)\,\mathrm{d}S,\;\;(\phi,\varphi),\,(v,v_{1})\in\mathcal{V}_{n}.

As a direct consequence, we have
D\mathcal{E}(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})=(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})
.

Now, we claim that the second Fréchet derivative
D^{2}\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{V}_{n}\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V}_{n},\mathcal{V}_{n}^{\prime})
of
\mathcal{E}
is given by

\displaystyle D^{2}\mathcal{E}(\phi,\varphi)[(v,h),(v_{1},h_{1})]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla v\cdot\nabla v_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}h\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}h_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi)vv_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon(1/K)\int_{\Gamma}(h-v)(h_{1}-v_{1})\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\int_{\Gamma}G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi)hh_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S,

for all
(v,h),\,(v_{1},h_{1}),\,(\phi,\varphi)\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
. In fact, let us fix
(v,h),\,(v_{1},h_{1}),\,(\phi,\varphi)\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
. We have,

\displaystyle D\mathcal{E}((\phi,\varphi)+(v,h))[(v_{1},h_{1})]-D\mathcal{E}(\phi,\varphi)[(v_{1},h_{1})]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla v\cdot\nabla v_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathcal{O}}[F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi+v)-F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi)]v_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}h\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}h_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\int_{\Gamma}[G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi+h)-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi)]h_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S+\varepsilon(1/K)\int_{\Gamma}(h-v)(h_{1}-v_{1})\,\mathrm{d}S.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}[F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi+v)-F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi)]v_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathcal{O}}vv_{1}\int_{0}^{1}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi+\tau v)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi)vv_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathcal{O}}vv_{1}\int_{0}^{1}[F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi+\tau v)-F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi)]\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}x

and

\int_{\Gamma}[G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi+h)-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi)]h_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S-\int_{\Gamma}G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi)hh_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S=\int_{\Gamma}hh_{1}\int_{0}^{1}[G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi+\tau h)-G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi)]\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}S.

Therefore, by the Hölder inequality and the fact that all norms are equivalent on the finite dimensional space
\mathcal{V}_{n}
, we infer that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{O}}vv_{1}\int_{0}^{1}[F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi+\tau v)-F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi)]\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\|v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\|v_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{0}^{1}\|F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi+\tau v)-F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C(n)\|(v,h)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{n}}\|(v_{1},h_{1})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{n}}\int_{0}^{1}\|F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi+\tau v)-F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Since
F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}
is continuous and bounded, an application of the DCT entails that the third factor on the RHS of the above inequality converges to zero as
(v,h)\to(0,0)
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{\|(v_{1},h_{1})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{n}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\mathcal{O}}vv_{1}\int_{0}^{1}[F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi+\tau v)-F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi)]\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}x\right|=o(\|(v,h)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{n}}).

Consequently,

\int_{\mathcal{O}}[F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi+v)-F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi)]v_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi)vv_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x+o(\|(v,h)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{n}}).

Similarly, we prove that

\int_{\Gamma}[G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi+h)-G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi)]h_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S=\int_{\Gamma}G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi)hh_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S+o(\|(v,h)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{n}}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle D\mathcal{E}((\phi,\varphi)+(v,h))[(v_{1},h_{1})]-D\mathcal{E}(\phi,\varphi)[(v_{1},h_{1})]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla v\cdot\nabla v_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}h\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}h_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S+\varepsilon(1/K)\int_{\Gamma}(h-v)(h_{1}-v_{1})\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi)vv_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\int_{\Gamma}G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi)hh_{1}\,\mathrm{d}S+o(\|(v,h)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{n}}).

This means that
D\mathcal{E}
is also Fréchet differentiable with the Fréchet derivative
D^{2}\mathcal{E}
defined as above. Besides, the first and second Fréchet derivatives
D\mathcal{E}
and
D^{2}\mathcal{E}
are both continuous and bounded on bounded subsets of
\mathcal{V}_{n}
due to the fact that
F_{\delta}^{\prime}
and
G_{\delta}^{\prime}
are Lipschitz-continuous,
F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}
and
G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}
are continuous and bounded.

We can now apply the Itô formula introduced in
[
19
, Theorem 4.32]
. Therefore, for every
t\in[0,T]
and
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\phi_{\delta,n}(t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}),\varphi_{\delta,n}(t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}))-\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-123.68387pt\hskip 123.68387pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-123.68387pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 123.68387pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-123.68387pt\hskip 123.68387pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-123.68387pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 123.68387pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.36386pt\hskip 101.36386pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-101.36386pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 101.36386pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-94.76591pt\hskip 94.76591pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-94.76591pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 94.76591pt\mathbb{V}}}}\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.26)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}(\phi_{n}(0),\varphi_{n}(0))+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}((\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n}))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{W})_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\bigg(\varepsilon\|\nabla F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{2}(U,\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\|\nabla_{\Gamma}F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}}G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle+\varepsilon[K]\|F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+\varepsilon[K]\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon[K]\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Gamma}(F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k})(F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k})\,\mathrm{d}S\bigg)\mathrm{d}s\bigg].

Next, by applying the Itô formula to the functional
\Phi(x)=\lvert x\rvert^{2}
, cf.
[
41
, Theorem 4.2.5]
,
since
(B_{1,n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n}),\phi_{\delta,n})=0
, we infer that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}(t\wedge\tau_{\kappa})\rvert^{2}+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.27)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\lvert\phi_{n}(0)\rvert^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}(\phi_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W).

Note that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-123.68387pt\hskip 123.68387pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-123.68387pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 123.68387pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-123.68387pt\hskip 123.68387pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-123.68387pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 123.68387pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-101.36386pt\hskip 101.36386pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-101.36386pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 101.36386pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\boldsymbol{b}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-94.76591pt\hskip 94.76591pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-94.76591pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 94.76591pt\mathbb{V}}}}=\mathchoice{{\hskip 10.3473pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle-B_{1}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})-A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n}),\mu_{\delta,n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.31277pt\hskip 151.31277pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-10.3473pt\hskip-151.31277ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 151.31277ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 10.3473pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle-B_{1}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})-A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n}),\mu_{\delta,n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-151.31277pt\hskip 151.31277pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-10.3473pt\hskip-151.31277ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 151.31277ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 9.4307pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle-B_{1}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})-A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n}),\mu_{\delta,n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-121.44661pt\hskip 121.44661pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-9.4307pt\hskip-121.44661ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 121.44661ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 9.4307pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle-B_{1}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})-A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n}),\mu_{\delta,n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-113.62671pt\hskip 113.62671pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-9.4307pt\hskip-113.62671ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 113.62671ptV_{1}}}}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 199.16928pt+\mathchoice{{\hskip 11.0973pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle-\mathcal{A}_{\varphi_{\delta,n}}(\theta_{\delta,n})-\tilde{B}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n}),\theta_{\delta,n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-138.22374pt\hskip 138.22374pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-11.0973pt\hskip-138.22374ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 138.22374ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 11.0973pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle-\mathcal{A}_{\varphi_{\delta,n}}(\theta_{\delta,n})-\tilde{B}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n}),\theta_{\delta,n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-138.22374pt\hskip 138.22374pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-11.0973pt\hskip-138.22374ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 138.22374ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 10.1807pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle-\mathcal{A}_{\varphi_{\delta,n}}(\theta_{\delta,n})-\tilde{B}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n}),\theta_{\delta,n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-115.33199pt\hskip 115.33199pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-10.1807pt\hskip-115.33199ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 115.33199ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 10.1807pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle-\mathcal{A}_{\varphi_{\delta,n}}(\theta_{\delta,n})-\tilde{B}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n}),\theta_{\delta,n}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-108.62021pt\hskip 108.62021pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-10.1807pt\hskip-108.62021ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 108.62021ptV_{\Gamma}}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left[\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\phi_{\delta,n}-M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\left[M_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}-\varphi_{\delta,n}\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\right]\mathrm{d}S,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}((\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{n}((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n}))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{W})_{\mathbb{H}}=\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}(\mu_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W)+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}(\theta_{\delta,n},F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}.

Plugging the two previous equalities into the RHS of (
4.26
), adding up side by side the resulting equality to (
4.27
), we deduce (
4.24
). This completes the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
∎

We now state and prove the following result.

Proposition 4.4
.

Suppose
(H1)
-
(H7)
are satisfied. Then, there exists a constant
C_{1,\delta}>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}}[\lvert D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}+\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n}(s))\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.28)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}[\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{1,\delta}[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}].

Furthermore, there exists a positive constant
C_{2,\delta}>0
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\leq C_{2,\delta}[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}].

(4.29)

Proof of Proposition
4.4
.

Let us proceed with estimating all the terms on the RHS of (
4.24
).

Using Assumption
(H2)
on
F_{1}
, i.e. (
3.6
), we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\leq\|\mathcal{S}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2})}^{2}\|F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}

(4.30)

\displaystyle\leq\|F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\leq\tilde{C}_{1}\lvert\mathcal{O}\rvert.

Once more, thanks to the assumption
(H2)
on
F_{1}
, we obtain for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\leq\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,V_{1})}^{2}\leq\|\mathcal{S}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{L}(V_{1})}^{2}\|F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,V_{1})}^{2}

(4.31)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O})\|F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,V_{1})}^{2}\leq C(C_{1},\tilde{C}_{1},\mathcal{O})(1+\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}).

Next, by (
4.31
) and the trace theorem, see
[
6
, Theorem 5.36]
, we infer

\displaystyle\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{O},\Gamma)\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\|_{V_{1}}^{2}

(4.32)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O},\Gamma)\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,V_{1})}^{2}\leq C(C_{1},\mathcal{O},\Gamma)(1+\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}).

Note that
J_{\delta}^{\prime}(s)\in(0,1)
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Thus,
\mathbb{A}_{\delta}^{\prime}(s)\in(0,\frac{1}{\delta})
. Therefore, by
(P4)
, we deduce that
\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(s)\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\delta}+\tilde{c}_{F}
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
. This, jointly with (
4.30
), implies that

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq(\delta^{-1}+\tilde{c}_{F})\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\leq C_{1}(\delta^{-1}+\tilde{c}_{F}).

(4.33)

For the first stochastically forced term in (
4.24
), we use the BDG inequality and (
4.30
). Thus, there exists
C=C(C_{1},\mathcal{O})
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(\mu_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\lvert\mu_{\delta,n}\lvert^{2}\|F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.34)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\lvert\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}.

By the assumption
(H2)
on
F_{2}
and arguing similarly as in (
4.30
) and (
4.31
), we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\|F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3},

(4.35)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\Gamma,n}F_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\Gamma,C_{3})(1+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}).

(4.36)

Therefore, by the BDG inequality, we infer that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\theta_{\delta,n},F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\lvert\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\|F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\Gamma,C_{2})\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\lvert\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now, we claim that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4C}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{C}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.37)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2C}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}M_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s+C,

where
C
may depend on
C_{1},C_{3},\,\,\mathcal{O},\,\Gamma,\,M_{0}
, and
N_{0}
.

Let us fix
(v,v_{\Gamma})\in\bigcup_{n}\mathcal{V}_{n}\subset\mathbb{V}
. Then from (
4.16
), we infer that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-85.92697pt\hskip 85.92697pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-85.92697pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 85.92697pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-85.92697pt\hskip 85.92697pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-85.92697pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 85.92697pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-69.27364pt\hskip 69.27364pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-69.27364pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 69.27364pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-64.89873pt\hskip 64.89873pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-64.89873pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 64.89873pt\mathbb{V}}}}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla v\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathcal{O}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})v\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}v_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n})v_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S+(1/K)\int_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n})(\varepsilon v_{\Gamma}-\varepsilon v)\,\mathrm{d}S.

Moreover, since
\mathbb{A}_{\delta}^{\prime}(s)\in(0,\frac{1}{\delta})
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbb{A}_{\delta}(0)=0
, we infer from
(P4)
that
\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(r)\rvert\leq\tilde{c}_{1,\delta}\lvert r\rvert
for all
r\in\mathbb{R}
, with
\tilde{c}_{1,\delta}=\tilde{c}_{F}+\delta^{-1}
. Therefore,

\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert\coloneq\|F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\tilde{c}_{1,\delta}\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert.

Analogously,

\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}\leq\tilde{c}_{2,\delta}\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma},\mbox{ with }\tilde{c}_{2,\delta}=\tilde{c}_{G}+\delta^{-1}.

Furthermore, by trace theorem, we have

\lvert v_{\Gamma}-v\rvert_{\Gamma}\leq(\lvert v_{\Gamma}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\lvert v\rvert_{\Gamma})\leq\lvert v_{\Gamma}\rvert_{\Gamma}+C(\Gamma,\mathcal{O})\|v\|_{V_{1}}.

Now from the previous observation and Hölder inequality, we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-85.92697pt\hskip 85.92697pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-85.92697pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 85.92697pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-85.92697pt\hskip 85.92697pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-85.92697pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 85.92697pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-69.27364pt\hskip 69.27364pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-69.27364pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 69.27364pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-64.89873pt\hskip 64.89873pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-64.89873pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 64.89873pt\mathbb{V}}}}\rvert\leq\varepsilon\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert\lvert\nabla v\rvert+\varepsilon^{-1}\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert v\rvert+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}^{-1}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}v_{\Gamma}\rvert_{\Gamma}

(4.38)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}^{-1}\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}\lvert v_{\Gamma}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\varepsilon[K]\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}\lvert v_{\Gamma}-v\rvert_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\leq C(\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert+\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+[K]\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma})\|(v,v_{\Gamma})\|_{\mathbb{V}},

where
C
may depend on
\tilde{c}_{G},\,\tilde{c}_{F},\,\mathcal{O},\,\Gamma,\,\varepsilon,\,\varepsilon_{\Gamma}
, and
\delta
.

On the other hand, by the trace theorem, see e.g.,
[
6
, Theorem 5.36]
, there exists a constant
C=C(\mathcal{O},\Gamma)>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}\coloneq\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{1}}.

Consequently,

\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\leq C(\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{1}}+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+(1/K)\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}).

(4.39)

Subsequently, notice that

\displaystyle\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}=\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-103.47435pt\hskip 103.47435pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-103.47435pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 103.47435pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-103.47435pt\hskip 103.47435pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-103.47435pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 103.47435pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-85.16728pt\hskip 85.16728pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-85.16728pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 85.16728pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n}),(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-80.6767pt\hskip 80.6767pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-80.6767pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 80.6767pt\mathbb{V}}}}\leq\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}(\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}+\|(\nabla\mu_{\delta,n},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O})\times\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{2}+\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\|(\nabla\mu_{\delta,n},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O})\times\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma)},

which, in turn, entails that

\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\leq\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{2}+2\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}(\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}).

(4.40)

It then follows from (
4.40
) that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sqrt{2}\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}(\times)

\displaystyle(\times)\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}+\sqrt{2}\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

This, jointly with the assumption
(H3)
and the Young inequality yields (
4.37
).

Thanks to (
4.37
) and (
4.39
), we infer that there exists a constant
C>0
, which may depend on
\tilde{c}_{G},\,\tilde{c}_{F},\,\mathcal{O},\,\Gamma,\,\varepsilon,\,\varepsilon_{\Gamma}
,
C_{1},\,C_{3},\,M_{0},\,N_{0}
,
and
\delta
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(\mu_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W\right)\right\rvert+\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\theta_{\delta,n},F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}\right\rvert

(4.41)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}M_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s+C

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}M_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}s+C.

Owing to
(H3)
, the Cauchy-Schwarz and Young inequalities, we find that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\lvert\sqrt{M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})}\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert\lvert\sqrt{M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})}\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\lvert\sqrt{M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})}\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\sqrt{M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})}\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\bar{M}_{0}\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}.

From (
4.35
), we infer that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Gamma}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\leq(\delta^{-1}+\tilde{c}_{G})\|F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\leq C_{3}(\delta^{-1}+\tilde{c}_{G}).

(4.42)

Thanks to (
4.32
) and (
4.35
), we infer that there exists
C=C(\mathcal{O},\Gamma,C_{1},C_{3})
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\lvert\int_{\Gamma}(F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k})(F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k})\,\mathrm{d}S\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\Gamma))}\|F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}

(4.43)

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}).

Considering (
4.3
), we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{0,n})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\tilde{c}_{\delta}(\lvert\mathcal{O}\rvert+\lvert\phi_{0,n}\rvert^{2})\leq\tilde{c}_{\delta}(\lvert\mathcal{O}\rvert+\lvert\phi_{0}\rvert^{2})\leq C(\delta,\mathcal{O})(1+\|\phi_{0}\|_{V_{1}}^{2}).

Analogously,

\|G_{\delta}(\varphi_{0,n})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq\tilde{c}_{\delta}(\lvert\Gamma\rvert+\lvert\varphi_{0,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2})\leq C(\delta,\Gamma)(1+\|\varphi_{0}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2}).

Therefore, there exists a constant
C=C(\varepsilon,\delta,\Gamma,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\mathcal{O})
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{n}(0),\varphi_{n}(0))\leq C(1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}).

Plugging the previous estimates in (
4.24
), taking the supremum over
[0,t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}]
and finally taking expectations with respect to
\mathbb{P}
, we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\kappa\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}[2\nu(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.44)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\Gamma}M_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\left[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}+\mathbb{E}(t\wedge\tau_{\kappa})+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq\tau}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right],

where the constant
C
depends on
\varepsilon,\,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\,C_{1},\,C_{3},\,\tilde{c}_{F},\,\tilde{c}_{G},\,\mathcal{O},\,\Gamma,\,K,\,M_{0}
and
\delta
.
Moreover, from the assumptions
(H3)
-
(H6)
and (
4.44
), we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\kappa\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}[2\nu_{\ast}\lvert D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.45)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\gamma_{\ast}\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}[M_{0}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+N_{0}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\left[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}+\mathbb{E}(t\wedge\tau_{\kappa})+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq\tau}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right],\;t\in[0,T].

Thus, by the Gronwall lemma, we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\kappa\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}[\lvert D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}+\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n}(s))\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{\kappa}}[\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}+|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}(s)|_{\Gamma}^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}],

where
C
depends on
\nu_{\ast},\,\varepsilon,\,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\,C_{1},\,C_{3},\,\tilde{c}_{F},\,\tilde{c}_{G},\,\mathcal{O},\,\Gamma,\,K,\,M_{0},\,\delta,\,\gamma_{\ast}
,
M_{0},\,N_{0}
, and
T
.

Moreover, since
\tau_{\kappa}\nearrow T

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
k\to\infty
, we infer (
4.28
), by passing to the limit in that previous inequality and by using the Fatou lemma.

Subsequently, by the Hölder inequality and the trace theorem, we obtain for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\leq 2\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+2\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\\
\leq 2\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+C(\mathcal{O},\Gamma)\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{1}}^{2}.

(4.46)

Finally, combining (
4.46
) and (
4.28
), we deduce (
4.29
).

∎

Proposition 4.5
.

Let Assumptions
(H1)
-
(H7)
hold. Assume
\delta\in(0,1)
and
r\geq 2
. Then, there exists a constant
C_{r/2,\delta}>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]^{r/2}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}

(4.47)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{Q_{t}}\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n}(s,x))\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}(s,x)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{r/2,\delta}[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{r}],\;\;t\in[0,T].

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C_{\delta,r}>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}(\tau)\rvert_{\Gamma}^{r}\leq C_{\delta,r}[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{r}],\;\;t\in[0,T].

(4.48)

Proof.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
. Without loss of generality, let us fix a number
r\geq 4
. Once we have proved Proposition
4.4
, each term of (
4.24
) is well defined, and therefore we do not need to use the stopping times anymore. Hence, by exploiting (
4.24
), raising the corresponding equality to the power
r/2
, taking the supremum in time over
[0,t]
,
t\in[0,T]
, and finally taking expectation with respect to
\mathbb{P}
, we infer that there exists a constant
c_{r}
depending on
\varepsilon,\,K,\,\varepsilon_{\Gamma}
, and
r
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(\tau),\varphi_{\delta,n}(\tau))]^{\frac{r}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left[2\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nu(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert D\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\right]\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}

(4.49)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{Q_{t}}\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+[M_{0}]^{\frac{r}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+[N_{0}]^{\frac{r}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{r}\bigg([\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{n}(0),\varphi_{n}(0))]^{\frac{r}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\|(F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}+F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{\frac{r}{2}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{t}(\|\nabla F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{2}(U,\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma}F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{\frac{r}{2}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{\frac{r}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\mu_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W)\right\rvert^{\frac{r}{2}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\theta_{\delta,n},(F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n}))\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}\right\rvert^{\frac{r}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\left\lvert\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}(F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k})(F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k})\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{\frac{r}{2}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left\lvert\int_{Q_{t}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\cdot\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{\frac{r}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\phi_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W)\right\rvert^{\frac{r}{2}}\bigg).

Let us control all the terms on the RHS of (
4.49
). First, by (
4.30
) and (
4.35
), we infer that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{0}^{t}(\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}+\|F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{r/2}\leq(C_{1}+C_{3})^{\frac{r}{2}}t^{\frac{r}{2}}.

Next, by the Hölder inequality, (
4.32
), and (
4.43
), we deduce that there exists
C=C(\mathcal{O},\Gamma,C_{1},C_{3},r)
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{r/2}+\left\lvert\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}(F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k})(F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k})\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{r/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{0}^{t}(1+\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\leq C\left[\int_{0}^{t}(1+\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{r})\,\mathrm{d}s\right]t^{\frac{r-2}{2}}.

Thanks to (
4.31
), (
4.36
), and the Hölder inequality, we deduce that there exists a constant
C
depending on
C_{1},\,C_{3},\,\mathcal{O},\,\Gamma
, and
r
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{0}^{t}(\|\nabla F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{2}(U,\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma}F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{r/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{0}^{t}(1+\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\leq C\left[\int_{0}^{t}(1+\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{r}+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{r})\,\mathrm{d}s\right]t^{\frac{r-2}{2}}.

By the Hölder and Young inequalities, together with the assumption
(H3)
, we deduce there exists
C=C(\bar{M}_{0},M_{0},r)
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{t}(M_{\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{\delta,n})\nabla\mu_{\delta,n},\nabla\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert^{r/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{r}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{r-2}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{[M_{0}]^{r/2}}{4}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+Ct^{\frac{r-2}{2}}\,\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{r}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Using the BDG inequality, (
4.30
), and (
4.35
), we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle c_{r}\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\mu_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W)\right\rvert^{r/2}+c_{r}\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\theta_{\delta,n},F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}\right\rvert^{r/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\|F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\|F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}.

Next, Note that as
(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\in H_{n}^{1}\times H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}
, then
\mu_{\delta,n}\in H_{n}^{1}
. From (
4.18
) and (
3.8
), we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mu_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}x\right\rvert=\lvert(\mathcal{S}_{n}[-\varepsilon\Delta\phi_{\delta,n}+\varepsilon^{-1}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})],1)\rvert=\lvert(-\varepsilon\Delta\phi_{\delta,n}+\varepsilon^{-1}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n}),\mathcal{S}_{n}1)\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert(-\varepsilon\Delta\phi_{\delta,n}+\varepsilon^{-1}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n}),1)\rvert\leq\varepsilon\lvert(\Delta\phi_{\delta,n},1)\rvert+\varepsilon^{-1}\lvert(F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n}),1)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{K}\int_{\Gamma}\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C(1+\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}),

where
C=C(K,\varepsilon,\mathcal{O},\Gamma,C_{F})
. This implies that

\left\lvert\fint_{\mathcal{O}}\mu_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}x\right\rvert\leq C(1+\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}).

(4.50)

Similarly, from (
3.9
) and (
4.18
), we deduce there exists a constant
C=C(K,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\mathcal{O},\Gamma,C_{G})
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\left\lvert\fint_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}S\right\rvert\leq C(1+\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\|G_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}).

(4.51)

Subsequently, owing to (
4.50
), (
4.51
), the Poincaré inequality, and the bulk-surface Poincaré inequality (
2.4
), we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\leq C\left(1+\|(\nabla\mu_{\delta,n},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\left\lvert\fint_{\mathcal{O}}\mu_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}x\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\fint_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\,\mathrm{d}S\right\rvert^{2}\right)

(4.52)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\|(\nabla\mu_{\delta,n},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}+\|G_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\right),

from which we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle c_{r}\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\mu_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W)\right\rvert^{r/2}+c_{r}\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\theta_{\delta,n},F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}\right\rvert^{r/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[t^{\frac{r}{4}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{4}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{4}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{r}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{r-2}{4}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 28.45274pt+t^{\frac{r-4}{4}}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{r/2}\,\mathrm{d}s+t^{\frac{r-4}{4}}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\|G_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}^{r/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right].

Furthermore, Young’s inequality leads to

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}\leq\frac{[M_{0}]^{r/2}}{4C}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{[N_{0}]^{r/2}}{2C}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+C(K,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\mathcal{O},\Gamma,C_{G},M_{0},N_{0},r),

with
C
being independent of
n
and
\delta
. Hence, there exists a positive constant
C
independent of
n
and
\delta
, but which may depend on
K,\,\varepsilon,\,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\,\mathcal{O},\,\Gamma,\,C_{G},\,C_{F},\,M_{0},\,N_{0}
, and
r
such that

\displaystyle c_{r}\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\mu_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W)\right\rvert^{r/2}+c_{r}\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\theta_{\delta,n},F_{2}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}\right\rvert^{r/2}

(4.53)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{[M_{0}]^{r/2}}{4}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+\frac{[N_{0}]^{r/2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+C(t^{\frac{r}{4}}+t^{\frac{r-2}{2}})

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{r}\,\mathrm{d}s+t^{\frac{r-4}{4}}C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}(\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{r/2}+\|G_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}^{r/2})\,\mathrm{d}s.

For the last stochastic integral in (
4.49
), we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\tau}(\phi_{\delta,n},F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W)\right\rvert^{r/2}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\|F_{1}(\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}\leq C\left[\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{r}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{r-2}{4}}\leq C(r,\mathcal{O},C_{1})+t^{\frac{r-2}{2}}\,\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert^{r}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Now, Plugging the previous estimates into the RHS of (
4.49
), using (
2.3
) and the assumptions
(H4)
-
(H6)
, we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]^{r/2}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}

(4.54)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{Q_{t}}\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[1+[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{n}(0),\varphi_{n}(0))]^{r/2}+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{t}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})]^{r/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg],

where
C
may depend on
r,\,\nu_{0},\,\lambda_{0},\,K,\,\varepsilon,\,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\,\mathcal{O},\,\Gamma,\,C_{G},\,C_{F},\,M_{0},\,N_{0},\,C_{\text{KN}}
, and
T
.

On the other hand, from (
4.33
) and (
4.42
), we infer that there exists
C=C(\mathcal{O},\Gamma,C_{1},C_{3},\tilde{c}_{F},\tilde{c}_{G},\delta,r)
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
