\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\lvert F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\leq C\,t^{\frac{r}{2}}.

On the one hand, note that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{n}(0),\varphi_{n}(0))]^{r/2}\leq C(1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{r}).

Consequently, there exists a positive constant
C
independent of
n
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]^{r/2}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{Q_{t}}\lambda(\phi_{\delta,n})\lvert\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{r}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq\tau}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s),\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))]^{r/2}\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg],\;\;t\in[0,T].

Hence, by applying the Gronwall inequality, we deduce (
4.47
). Subsequently, considering (
4.46
) and (
4.47
), we infer (
4.48
). This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
. For every
\epsilon>0
and every
\eta>0
, there exists
\kappa>0
such that for every sequence
(\tau_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathbb{F}
-stopping times with
\tau_{n}\leq T
,

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0\leq\theta\leq\kappa}\mathbb{P}\left(\{\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}(\tau_{n}+\theta)-\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}(\tau_{n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\geq\eta\}\right)\leq\epsilon.

(4.55)

Proof.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
and
\theta>0
. Let
(\tau_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
\mathbb{F}
-stopping times such that
0\leq\tau_{n}\leq T
. Let
\eta,\,\epsilon>0
be fixed and
\kappa>0
to be chosen later. Then, from (
4.21
), we infer

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}(\tau_{n}+\theta)-\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}(\tau_{n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\leq\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|\mathbf{b}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{W}\right\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|B_{1,n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|\mathcal{S}_{n}A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi_{\delta,n}}(\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{\Gamma}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|\tilde{B}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{\Gamma}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W\right\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}+\left\|\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma}\right\|_{V_{\Gamma}^{\prime}}.

Next, by the Hölder inequality, (
2.10
), (
4.28
), and (
4.47
), we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|B_{1,n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|B_{1,n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\|B_{1,n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\phi_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\phi_{\delta,n}(s)\|_{V_{1}}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{\sigma}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\phi_{\delta,n}(s)\|_{V_{1}}^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{\sigma}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By (
2.13
), the assumption
(H3)
, (
4.28
), and (
4.47
), we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|\mathcal{S}_{n}A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathcal{S}_{n}A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathcal{S}_{n}A_{\phi_{\delta,n}}(\mu_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\left(\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}(s)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Analogously, by (
2.13
) and
(H3)
in conjunction with (
4.28
) and (
4.47
), we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi_{\delta,n}}(\theta_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{V_{\Gamma}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Next, owing to (
2.12
), (
4.47
), and (
4.48
), we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|\tilde{B}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{\Gamma}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\tilde{B}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{\Gamma}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\|\tilde{B}_{n}(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{V_{\Gamma}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\varphi_{\delta,n}(s)\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{\sigma}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\varphi_{\delta,n}(s)\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{\sigma}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\theta^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using the Itô isometry, (
4.30
) and (
4.35
), we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s))\,\mathrm{d}W(s)\right\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}+\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma}(s)\right\|_{V_{\Gamma}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s))\,\mathrm{d}W(s)\right\|_{V_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left[\mathbb{E}\left\|\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))\,\mathrm{d}W_{\Gamma}(s)\right\|_{V_{\Gamma}^{\prime}}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,V_{1}^{\prime})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left[\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,V_{\Gamma}^{\prime})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|F_{1,n}(\phi_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left[\mathbb{E}\int_{\tau_{n}}^{\tau_{n}+\theta}\|F_{2,n}(\varphi_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq([\lvert\mathcal{O}\rvert C_{1}]^{1/2}+[\lvert\Gamma\rvert C_{2}]^{1/2})\theta^{1/2}.

It then follows from the previous inequalities that there exists a constant
c_{1}>0
independent of
n
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}(\tau_{n}+\theta)-\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}(\tau_{n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\leq c_{1}\theta^{1/2}.

Taking
\kappa=\left(\frac{\eta\epsilon}{c_{1}}\right)^{2}
, then, by the Chebychev inequality we deduce (
4.55
).
∎

Here, given a Banach space
X
, the notation
X_{w}
indicates that the space
X
is endowed with the weak topology.
We now introduce the following topological space

\mathfrak{X}_{T}\coloneq\mathfrak{X}_{T,2}\times\mathfrak{X}_{T,1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma},

(4.56)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{T,2}=C([0,T];\mathbb{H})\cap C([0,T];\mathbb{V}_{w})\cap L_{w}^{4}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{2})\cap L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{V}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{T,1}=C([0,T];U_{0})\mbox{ and }\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma}=C([0,T];U_{0}^{\Gamma}).

We recall that we can find a set
\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{F}
of measure zero such that
W(t,\cdot,\omega)\in C([0,T];U_{0})
and
W_{\Gamma}(t,\cdot,\omega)\in C([0,T];U_{0}^{\Gamma})
for any
\omega\in\Omega\backslash\Omega_{0}
. In the sequel, we use the notations
\mathcal{L}(W)
and
\mathcal{L}(W_{\Gamma})
to denote the laws of
W=(W(t))_{t\in[0,T]}
and
W_{\Gamma}=(W_{\Gamma}(t))_{t\in[0,T]}
on
C([0,T];U_{0})
and on
C([0,T];U_{0}^{\Gamma})
, respectively.
For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we consider the constant sequences of cylindrical Wiener processes

W_{1,n}\equiv W\quad\text{and}\quad W_{2,n}\equiv W_{\Gamma},

and we construct a family of probability laws on
\mathfrak{X}_{T,1}=C([0,T];U_{0})
and on
\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma}=C([0,T];U_{0}^{\Gamma})
by setting

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}(\cdot)

\displaystyle\coloneq\mathbb{P}(W_{1,n}\in\cdot)\in P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,1}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\Gamma,n}(\cdot)

\displaystyle\coloneq\mathbb{P}(W_{2,n}\in\cdot)\in P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma}),

where
P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,1})
and
P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma})
denote the set of all probability measures on the measurable spaces
(\mathfrak{X}_{T,1},\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,1}))
and
(\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma},\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma}))
, respectively.

We have the following important result.

Proposition 4.7
.

Assume
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then, the sequence of laws of
((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n}),W_{1,n},W_{2,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight on
\mathfrak{X}_{T}
.

Proof.

First, the family of probability laws
\{\mathbb{P}_{n}\}_{n}
,
\{\mathbb{P}_{\Gamma,n}\}_{n}
is tight in
P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,1})
, and in
P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma})
, respectively. Indeed, if we endow the space
\mathfrak{X}_{T,1}
with the uniform convergence norm, it becomes a Polish space. By
[
9
, Theorem 6.8]
,
P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,1})
endowed with the Prohorov metric is a separable and complete metric space. Moreover, by construction, the family of probability laws
\{\mathbb{P}_{n}\}_{n}
is reduced to one element which is the law
\mathcal{L}(W)
of
W
and belongs to
P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,1})
. Therefore, by
[
44
, Theorem 3.2, Chapter II]
we deduce that the family
(\mathbb{P}_{n})_{n}
is tight on
P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,1})
. Similarly, we can prove that the family
(\mathbb{P}_{\Gamma,n})_{n}
is tight on
P_{r}(\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma})
.

Next, thanks to the propositions
4.4
and
4.5
, the lemmas
A.2
and
4.6
, we can apply
[
13
, Corollary 3.9]
and deduce that the sequence of laws of
\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\coloneq(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})
is tight. This completes the proof of Proposition
4.7
.
∎

4.4.
Convergence to a weak solution

In this section, we prove that there exists a martingale solution to the approximate system (
4.4a
)-(
4.7a
), with
\delta
fixed.
For a
\mathfrak{X}_{T}
-valued r.v.
X
, we denote by
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{X}_{T}}(\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}})
its law in
\mathfrak{X}_{T}
. We state the following auxiliary result.

Proposition 4.8
.

Assume
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then, there exists a subsequence
(n_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, a complete probability space
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
, and on this space,
\mathfrak{X}_{T}
-valued random variables
((\bar{\phi}_{\delta,k},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,k}),\bar{W}_{1,k},\bar{W}_{2,k})
and
((\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}),\bar{W},\bar{W}_{\Gamma})
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{X}_{T}}(((\bar{\phi}_{\delta,k},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,k}),\bar{W}_{1,k},\bar{W}_{2,k}))=\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{X}_{T}}(((\phi_{\delta,n_{k}},\varphi_{\delta,n_{k}}),W_{1,n_{k}},W_{2,n_{k}})),

(4.57)

\displaystyle((\bar{\phi}_{\delta,k},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,k}),\bar{W}_{1,k},\bar{W}_{2,k})\to((\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}),\bar{W},\bar{W}_{\Gamma})\mbox{ in }\mathfrak{X}_{T}\mbox{ as }k\to\infty,\;\;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-a.s.}

(4.58)

Before we embark on proving the above result, let us emphasize that (
4.58
) yields that

(\bar{W}_{1,k},\bar{W}_{2,k})\to(\bar{W},\bar{W}_{\Gamma})\mbox{ in }C([0,T];U_{0}\times U_{0}^{\Gamma})\mbox{ as }k\to\infty,\;\;\bar{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-a.s.}.

(4.59)

We will continue to denote the above sequences by
((\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n}),\bar{W}_{1,n},\bar{W}_{2,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
((\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n_{k}}),W_{1,n},W_{2,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, for the sake of brevity.

Proof.

First, arguing as in
[
13
]
, Proof of Corollary 3.12, we can check that the space
\mathfrak{X}_{T,2}
satisfies Assumption
(10)
in Jakubowski’s paper
[
36
]
. We point out that
\mathfrak{X}_{T,2}
is in fact the product of countable spaces satisfying the assumption
(10)
in
[
36
]
. Secondly, since
\mathfrak{X}_{T,1}\times\mathfrak{X}_{T,\Gamma}
is a Polish space, its satisfies the assumption
(10)
in
[
36
]
. Therefore, we can apply
[
36
, Theorem 2]
and thus deduce all the conclusions in the proposition. The proof of Proposition
4.8
is now complete.
∎

Remark 4.9
.

(a)
Proposition
4.8
yields that there exists a new probability space
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
and a sequence of random variables
X_{n}:(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}})\to(\Omega,\mathcal{F})
such that the law of
X_{n}
is
\mathbb{P}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, i.e.
\mathbb{P}=\bar{\mathbb{P}}\circ X_{n}^{-1}
, so that composition with
X_{n}
preserves laws, and the convergences (
4.58
) and (
4.59
) hold.

(b)
Notice that from the proof of Skorokhod-Jakubowski Theorem, see
[
36
]
, possibly enlarging the new probability space, we may assume that the new probability space
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
is independent of
\delta
.

Let us consider a sequence of processes
(\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n})_{n}\,,(\bar{\mu}_{\delta,n})_{n}
, and
(\bar{\theta}_{\delta,n})_{n}
defined by

\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}\coloneq\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n}\circ X_{n},\;\;\bar{\mu}_{\delta,n}\coloneq\mu_{\delta,n}\circ X_{n},\;\;\bar{\theta}_{\delta,n}\coloneq\theta_{\delta,n}\circ X_{n},

(4.60)

which enjoy the same properties as for the sequences
(\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}_{\delta,n})_{n}\,,(\mu_{\delta,n})_{n}
, and
(\theta_{\delta,n})_{n}
, since the map
X_{n}
preserves laws.

In the following, let us choose and fix
r\geq 2
. By (
4.57
) and (
4.58
) in Proposition
4.8
, recalling the estimates in Propositions
4.5
and
A.1
, and in Lemma
A.2
, using also the Banach-Alaoglu Theorem and the Vitali convergence theorem, we have up to a subsequence still labeled by the same subscript,

\displaystyle(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\to(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{\ell}(\bar{\Omega};C([0,T];\mathbb{H}))\cap L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}([0,T];\mathbb{V})),\;\;\forall\ell<r,

(4.61)

\displaystyle(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\rightharpoonup}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{V})),

\displaystyle(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\rightharpoonup(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{4}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{2})),

\displaystyle(\bar{\mu}_{\delta,n},\bar{\theta}_{\delta,n})\rightharpoonup(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{V}))\cap L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{4}(0,T;\mathbb{H})),

\displaystyle(\bar{W}_{1,n},\bar{W}_{2,n})\to(\bar{W},\bar{W}_{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{\ell}(\bar{\Omega};C([0,T];U_{0}\times U_{0}^{\Gamma})),\;\;\forall\ell<r,

\displaystyle\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}\rightharpoonup\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;V)),

\displaystyle\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}\lvert_{\Gamma}\rightharpoonup\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\lvert_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))).

Moreover, from the strong convergences of
\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}
and
\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n}
above, we have, modulo the extraction of a subsequence (still) denoted
(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
:

(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\to(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\mbox{-a.e. in }Q_{T}\times\tilde{\Omega}.

(4.62)

We have the following important auxiliary result.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}_{\delta}=(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})
be the stochastic process as given by (
4.61
). Then, there exists a subsequence of
\mathfrak{X}_{\delta,n}\coloneq(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})
and
\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{n}\coloneq(\bar{W}_{1,n},\bar{W}_{2,n})^{tr}
still labeled by the
same subscript, such that as
n\to\infty
,

\int_{0}^{t}\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{n}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta,n}(s))\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathcal{W}}^{n}(s)\to\int_{0}^{t}\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta}(s))\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathcal{W}}(s)\mbox{ in probability in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H}),

(4.63)

for all
t\in[0,T]
, with
\bar{\mathcal{W}}\coloneq(\bar{W},\bar{W}_{\Gamma})^{tr}
.

We report the proof of Proposition
4.10
in appendix
B
.

To reach the limit in the weak formulation of the finite-dimensional problem (
4.2
)-(
4.16
), in addition to the previous convergences, we will need the following convergences. These follow from the strong convergences of
(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})_{n}
and
(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})_{n}
, see (
4.61
), and the fact that
F_{\delta}^{\prime}
and
G_{\delta}^{\prime}
are Lipschitz-continuous. Thus, as
n\to\infty
along a non-relabeled subsequence, we get

\displaystyle F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\to F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))),

(4.64)

\displaystyle G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\to G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))).

Moreover, by the pointwise convergences (
4.62
) and the continuity of the maps
M_{\mathcal{O}},\,M_{\Gamma},\,\nu,\,\lambda
, and
\gamma
, we infer that, as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})

\displaystyle\to\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}),\quad\lambda(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\to\lambda(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}),\quad M_{\mathcal{O}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\to M_{\mathcal{O}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}),\quad\mathrm{d}\mathbb{\bar{P}}\mbox{-a.e. in}\quad Q_{T}

(4.65)

\displaystyle M_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})

\displaystyle\to M_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}),\quad\gamma(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\to\gamma(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}),\quad\mathrm{d}\mathbb{\bar{P}}\mbox{-a.e. on}\quad\Sigma_{T}.

Now, from the convergence results established in this section and the DCT, we can pass to the limit in the weak formulation of (
4.4a
)-(
4.7a
), to deduce that the process
(\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta},\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta},\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})
satisfies
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. and for all test functions

\displaystyle\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\in\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}V^{n}\subset V,\;\;\upsilon\in\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H_{n}^{1}\subset V_{1},\;\;\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\in\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}H_{\Gamma,n}^{1}\subset V_{\Gamma},\;\;(\psi,\psi\lvert_{\Gamma})\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{V}_{n}\subset\mathbb{V},

\displaystyle 2\int_{Q_{t}}\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}:D\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{Q_{t}}\lambda(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\gamma(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s-\int_{Q_{t}}\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s,\;\;t\in[0,T],

(4.66)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\hskip 10.3473pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(t),\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-38.14868pt\hskip 38.14868pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-10.3473pt\hskip-38.14868ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 38.14868ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 10.3473pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(t),\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-38.14868pt\hskip 38.14868pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-10.3473pt\hskip-38.14868ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 38.14868ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 9.4307pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(t),\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-32.51404pt\hskip 32.51404pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-9.4307pt\hskip-32.51404ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 32.51404ptV_{1}}}}{{\hskip 9.4307pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(t),\upsilon\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-30.756pt\hskip 30.756pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-9.4307pt\hskip-30.756ptV_{1}^{\prime}\hskip 30.756ptV_{1}}}}+\int_{Q_{t}}[-\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\upsilon+M_{\mathcal{O}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\upsilon]\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s=(\phi_{0},\upsilon)+\left(\int_{0}^{t}F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\,\mathrm{d}\bar{W},\upsilon\right),

(4.67)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\hskip 11.0973pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(t),\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-46.37093pt\hskip 46.37093pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-11.0973pt\hskip-46.37093ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 46.37093ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 11.0973pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(t),\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-46.37093pt\hskip 46.37093pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-11.0973pt\hskip-46.37093ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 46.37093ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 10.1807pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(t),\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-39.94466pt\hskip 39.94466pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-10.1807pt\hskip-39.94466ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 39.94466ptV_{\Gamma}}}}{{\hskip 10.1807pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(t),\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-38.18661pt\hskip 38.18661pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-10.1807pt\hskip-38.18661ptV_{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 38.18661ptV_{\Gamma}}}}+\int_{\Sigma_{t}}[-\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}+M_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}]\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad=(\varphi_{0},\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}F_{2}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\,\mathrm{d}\bar{W}_{\Gamma},\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\right)_{\Gamma},

(4.68)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}\bar{\mu}_{\delta}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}\psi\lvert_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S-\varepsilon\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\psi\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathcal{O}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\psi\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad=\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\lvert_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\int_{\Gamma}G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\psi\lvert_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S+(1/K)\int_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}-\bar{\phi}_{\delta})(\varepsilon\psi\lvert_{\Gamma}-\varepsilon\psi)\,\mathrm{d}S.

(4.69)

Note that (
4.4
)-(
4.4
) also hold for all
\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\in V,\,\upsilon\in V_{1},\,\upsilon\lvert_{\Gamma}\in V_{\Gamma}
, and
(\psi,\psi\lvert_{\Gamma})\in\mathbb{V}
by a density argument.

5.
Uniform estimates with respect to delta

In this section, we derive additional uniform estimates that are independent of
\delta
.

We start with the following inequalities. First, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert(M_{\mathcal{O}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta,n},\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{\bar{M}_{0}}{2}\lvert\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}.

This, in conjunction with (
4.24
), (
4.54
), and the fact that
\mathbb{P}=\bar{\mathbb{P}}\circ X_{n}^{-1}
for every
n
, that is,
X_{n}
, preserves laws, yields that for every
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(t),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n}(t))]+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}[2\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\lvert D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lambda(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\lvert\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}]\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

(5.1)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{\bar{E}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\gamma(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\lvert\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{\bar{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{\bar{E}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}M_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\phi_{n}(0),\varphi_{n}(0))+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{\bar{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{1,n}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}C_{2}}{2}\mathbb{\bar{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\bar{M}_{0}}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon C_{1}}{2}\right)\mathbb{\bar{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\varepsilon(1/K)\mathbb{\bar{E}}\int_{0}^{t}(\|F_{2,n}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+\|F_{1,n}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2})\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\mathbb{\bar{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\lvert F_{1,n}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\mathbb{\bar{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Gamma}G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\lvert F_{2,n}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

and for every
t\in[0,T]
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(s),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n}(s))]^{\frac{r}{2}}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}

(5.2)

\displaystyle+\bar{\bar{\mathbb{E}}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\leq C\bigg[1+[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{n}(0),\bar{\varphi}_{n}(0))]^{\frac{r}{2}}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(s),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n}(s))]^{\frac{r}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{1,n}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{2,n}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\bigg].

The next step now is to pass to the limit by letting
n\to\infty
in (
5.1
) and (
5.2
).

Let us consider (
5.1
). By the Lipschitz continuity of the maps
F_{1,n}
,
F_{2,n}
, and similar reasoning as in (
B.1
), we infer that as
n\to\infty
,

(F_{1,n}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}),F_{2,n}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n}))\to(F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}),F_{2}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}))\mbox{ in }L^{\ell}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{U};\mathbb{H})))\quad\forall\ell<r.

(5.3)

Next, since
F_{\delta}
and
G_{\delta}
are continuous and satisfy the linear growth condition, see (
4.3
), and since

\phi_{0,n}\to\phi_{0}\mbox{ in }V_{1}\mbox{ and }\varphi_{0,n}\to\varphi_{0}\mbox{ in }V_{\Gamma},

(5.4)

we deduce that
F_{\delta}(\phi_{0,n})\to F_{\delta}(\phi_{0})
and
G_{\delta}(\varphi_{0,n})\to G_{\delta}(\varphi_{0})
as
n\to\infty
.

Moreover, thanks to the embedding
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\Gamma)
, we infer that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\varphi_{0,n}-\phi_{0,n})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\leq\lvert\varphi_{0,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+\lvert\phi_{0,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\leq\lvert\varphi_{0}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+C(\mathcal{O})\|\phi_{0}\|_{V_{1}}^{2}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\lvert\nabla\phi_{0,n}\rvert^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}{2}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{0,n}\rvert^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2K}\lvert\varphi_{0,n}-\phi_{0,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\leq C(\varepsilon,\mathcal{O},K)\|\phi_{0}\|_{V_{1}}^{2}+(\varepsilon_{\Gamma}+\varepsilon(1/K))\|\varphi_{\Gamma}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{n}(0),\bar{\varphi}_{n}(0))\leq C(\varepsilon,\mathcal{O},K)\|\phi_{0}\|_{V_{1}}^{2}+(\varepsilon_{\Gamma}+\varepsilon(1/K))\|\varphi_{\Gamma}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|F_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{n}(0))\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 99.58464pt+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\|G_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{n}(0))\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}.

In addition, to pass to the limit in (
5.1
), we rely on the following weak convergences,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{2}(\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}),

(5.5)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\lambda(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})}\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\sqrt{\lambda(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})}\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }\mathbb{L}^{2}(\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{M_{\mathcal{O}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})}\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta,n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\sqrt{M_{\mathcal{O}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})}\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta}

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }\mathbb{L}^{2}(\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{\gamma(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})}\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\sqrt{\gamma(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})}\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }\mathbb{L}^{2}(\bar{\Omega}\times\Sigma_{T}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{M_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})}\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta,n}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\sqrt{M_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})}\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }\mathbb{L}^{2}(\bar{\Omega}\times\Sigma_{T}).

Since
(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\to(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})
in
C([0,T];\mathbb{H})
,
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s., cf. Proposition
4.8
, by continuity of the map
\nu:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, we deduce that up to a subsequence,
\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})}\to\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})}

\mathrm{d}\mathbb{\bar{P}}
-a.e. in
Q_{T}
, as
n\to\infty
. Moreover, since the
\mu
-measure of
\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}
is finite, the Severini–Egorov Theorem ensures that, for every
\epsilon>0
, there exists a measurable subset
\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}
of
\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}
s.t.
\mu(\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}^{c})<\epsilon
, and
\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})}\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}}}\underset{n\to\infty}{\to}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})}\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}}}
in
L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\bar{\Omega})
.
Here
\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}^{c}=(\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T})\backslash\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}
.

Let
\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\in\left\{A=(A_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq d}):\,A_{ij}\in\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}V_{\sigma}^{n}\right\}
and
\chi\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\bar{\Omega})
.
Note that

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}))}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}):\chi(t,\bar{\omega})\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(t,x,\bar{\omega})

\displaystyle=\int_{\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}))}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}):\chi(t,\bar{\omega})\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}(x)\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(t,x,\bar{\omega})

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}))}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}):\chi(t,\bar{\omega})\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}(x)\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}^{c}_{\epsilon}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(t,x,\bar{\omega}).

Now, since
\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})}\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon}}}\underset{n\to\infty}{\to}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})}\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon}}}
in
L^{\infty}(\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T})
,

\nabla\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}\underset{n\to\infty}{\rightharpoonup}\nabla\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}
in
L^{2}(\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
, we infer that

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}))}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}):\chi(t,\bar{\omega})\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}(x)\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(t,x,\bar{\omega})

\displaystyle\underset{n\to\infty}{\to}\int_{\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(t,x,\bar{\omega}))}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}(t,x,\bar{\omega}):\chi(t,\bar{\omega})\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}(x)\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(t,x,\bar{\omega}).

Fix
\epsilon>0
. By the Hölder inequality, the fact that
\nabla\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}
is uniformly bounded w.r.t.
n
in
L^{2}(\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
, we deduce that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}))}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}):\chi(t,\bar{\omega})\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}(x)\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}^{c}_{\epsilon}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(t,x,\bar{\omega})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq[\bar{\nu}_{0}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\|D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}\|_{L^{2}(\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})}\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\bar{\Omega})}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})}[\mu(\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}^{c})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}.

Subsequently, since
\epsilon
is arbitrary, we infer that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lvert\int_{\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}))}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}):\chi(t,\bar{\omega})\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}(x)\lvert_{1_{\mathcal{B}^{c}_{\epsilon}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(t,x,\bar{\omega})\right\rvert=0.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}))}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta,n}(t,x,\bar{\omega}):\chi(t,\bar{\omega})\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(t,x,\bar{\omega})

\displaystyle=\int_{\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}}\sqrt{\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(t,x,\bar{\omega}))}D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}(t,x,\bar{\omega}):\chi(t,\bar{\omega})\mbox{\boldmath{$v$}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(t,x,\bar{\omega}).

Due to density, the last equality holds for all test functions in the space
\mathbb{L}^{2}(\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
.

This completes the proof of the first part of condition (
5.5
).

The proof of the other part can be proven similarly with straightforward modifications.

Next, exploiting the previous convergences results and weak lower-semicontinuity of the norm, we deduce that for all
t\in[0,T]
and for every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(t),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(t))]+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}[2\nu(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\lvert D\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}+\lambda(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\lvert\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\gamma(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\lvert\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

(5.6)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\lvert\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}M_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon,\mathcal{O},K)\|\phi_{0}\|_{V_{1}}^{2}+(\varepsilon_{\Gamma}+\varepsilon(1/K))\|\varphi_{\Gamma}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\|G_{\delta}(\varphi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+2\varepsilon(1/K)\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\varepsilon(1/K)\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{2}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}C_{2}}{2}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\lvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\left(\frac{\bar{M}_{0}}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon C_{1}}{2}\right)\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\lvert F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Gamma}G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\lvert F_{2}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s.

Let us now consider (
5.2
). We choose and fix
k\geq 1
. By (
4.61
)-1) and (
5.3
), we infer that

\displaystyle\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{1,n}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\underset{n\to\infty}{\to}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\mbox{ a.e. in }\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T},

(5.7)

\displaystyle\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{2,n}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\underset{n\to\infty}{\to}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{2}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\mbox{ a.e. in }\bar{\Omega}\times\Sigma_{T}.

Moreover, as in (
4.33
) and (
4.42
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{1,n}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Gamma}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{2,n}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle\leq(\delta^{-1}+\tilde{c}_{F})C_{1}+(\delta^{-1}+\tilde{c}_{G})C_{3}.

Hence, by the DCT, we deduce that as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{1,n}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}

\displaystyle\to\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\rvert\lvert F_{2,n}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}

\displaystyle\to\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{2}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}.

We recall that
\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}\to\bar{\phi}_{\delta}
in
C([0,T];H_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{O}))
,
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s., see Proposition
4.8
. So, using Corollary E.3 and Proposition E.1 from
[
12
]
, and the Fatou lemma, we deduce that

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\lvert\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s)\rvert^{r}=\int_{\bar{\Omega}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\lvert\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s,\bar{\omega})\rvert^{r}\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{\omega})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\bar{\Omega}}\liminf_{n\to\infty}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\lvert\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(s,\bar{\omega})\rvert^{r}\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{\omega})

(5.8)

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\bar{\Omega}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\lvert\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(s,\bar{\omega})\rvert^{r}\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{\omega}).

In a similar way, we show that

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(s))]^{r/2}\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\bar{\Omega}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}(s,\bar{\omega}),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n}(s,\bar{\omega}))]^{r/2}\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathbb{P}}(\bar{\omega}).

From now on, recalling (
4.61
) and making use of the previous observations, we can then pass to the limit by letting
n\to\infty
in (
5.2
) to deduce that for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(s))]^{r/2}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\right]

(5.9)

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[1+\|\phi_{0}\|_{V_{1}}^{r}+\|\varphi_{\Gamma}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{r}+\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{r/2}+\|G_{\delta}(\varphi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}^{r/2}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(s))]^{r/2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{p/2}\bigg],

where the constant
C
is independent of
\delta
.

Let us consider the terms
\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}
and
\|G_{\delta}(\varphi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}
. From
(P1)
and
(P3)
, we infer that

\|F_{\delta}(\phi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\|F(\phi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}.

Analogously,

\|G_{\delta}(\varphi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq\|G(\varphi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}.

Now, we consider the last two terms on the RHS of (
5.9
). We observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s,x))\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s,x))e_{1,k}(x)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s,x))\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s,x))e_{1,k}(x)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s))\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\,\mathrm{d}s\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{t})}^{2}\leq\text{meas}(Q_{t})\tilde{C}_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\|F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s))\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\,\mathrm{d}s.

This, together with the assumption (
3.8
) on
F^{{\prime\prime}}
implies that

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s,x))\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s,x))e_{1,k}(x)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s))\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\,\mathrm{d}s\right),

for a certain constant
C
which is independent of
\delta
. Next, Hölder’s inequality leads to

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{t}}\lvert F^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\leq C\left[1+t^{\frac{r-2}{2}}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{r/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right].

Similarly, we deduce that there exists a constant
C
independent of
\delta
such that

\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\lvert G^{{\prime\prime}}_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{2}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\leq C\left[1+t^{\frac{r-2}{2}}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\|G_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}^{r/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right],\;t\in[0,T].

Plugging the above estimates into the RHS of (
5.9
), we are led to

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(s))]^{r/2}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}(s)\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta}(s)\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}

(5.10)

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}(s)\rvert^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\bigg]\leq C\bigg[1+\|\phi_{0}\|_{V_{1}}^{r}+\|\varphi_{\Gamma}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{r}+\|F(\phi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{r/2}

\displaystyle+\|G(\varphi_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}^{r/2}+\bar{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{0\leq r\leq s}[\mathcal{E}_{tot}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(r),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(r))]^{r/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg],\;\;t\in[0,T].

Let us point out that the constant
C
in (
5.10
) does not depend on
\delta
. Therefore, by applying the Gronwall lemma, we deduce that there exists a positive constant
c_{r}
, with
c_{r}
being independent of
\delta
such that

\displaystyle\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{L}^{2}(\Gamma)))}

\displaystyle\leq c_{r},

(5.11)

\displaystyle\|\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;V))}

\displaystyle\leq c_{r},

\displaystyle\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;V_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq c_{r},

\displaystyle\|(F_{\delta}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}),G_{\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}))\|_{L^{r/2}(\bar{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathcal{O})\times L^{1}(\Gamma)))}

\displaystyle\leq c_{r},

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O})))}

\displaystyle\leq c_{r},

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H}))}

\displaystyle\leq c_{r},

\displaystyle\|\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}-\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma)))}

\displaystyle\leq c_{r}.

Moreover, owing to (
4.4
) and (
5.11
), and arguing similarly as in (
4.52
), we deduce there exists a positive constant
c_{r}
independent of
\delta
such that

\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H}))}\leq c_{r}.

(5.12)

Furthermore, from (
5.11
) and similar reasoning as in (
4.46
), we deduce that

\|\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma)))}\leq c_{r}.

(5.13)

On the other hand, from the assumption
(H2)
, the estimates (
5.11
), and (
5.13
), we get

\|(F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}),F_{2}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}))\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\bar{\Omega};\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{U};\mathbb{H}))\cap L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{U};\mathbb{V})))}\leq c_{r}.

(5.14)

Let us now prove that
(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})_{\delta}
satisfies the following property: For every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\leq c_{r}.

(5.15)

The proof is given in four steps.

Step1:
Since the sequence
(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})_{n}
enjoys the same property as
(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})_{n}
for each
\delta>0
, then from (
A.5
) and (
A.7
), we infer that there exists
C=C(K,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\mathcal{O},\Gamma)
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\delta>0
,

\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\leq C(\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta,n},\bar{\theta}_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n})\rvert^{2}+\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}).

(5.16)

From the previous inequality, the proposition
4.8
and the weak lower semicontinuity of the norm together with the assumption
(H7)
, we deduce that for every
\delta>0
,

\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\leq c_{\ast}(\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert^{2}+\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}).

(5.17)

Step2:
Let
G_{\delta}^{\prime}(r)\coloneq\mathbb{A}_{\Gamma,\delta}(r)-\tilde{c}_{G}r,\,r\in\mathbb{R}
, where
\mathbb{A}_{\Gamma,\delta}
is the regularized Yosida of
\mathbb{A}_{\Gamma}=G^{\prime}+\tilde{c}_{G}I
.
Since
\lvert\mathbb{A}_{\Gamma,\delta}(\cdot)\rvert\leq\lvert\mathbb{A}_{\Gamma}(\cdot)\rvert
for every
\delta>0
, cf.
[
7
, Chapter II, Proposition 1.1]
, we infer that for every
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})|\leq\lvert\mathbb{A}_{\Gamma,\delta}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert+\tilde{c}_{G}\lvert\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\rvert\leq\lvert\mathbb{A}_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert+\tilde{c}_{G}\lvert\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\rvert

(5.18)

\displaystyle\leq\lvert G^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert+2\tilde{c}_{G}\lvert\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\rvert\leq c_{G^{\prime}}+c_{G^{\prime}}\lvert\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\rvert^{q-1}+2\tilde{c}_{G}\lvert\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\rvert\leq C(q,c_{G^{\prime}},\tilde{c}_{G})(1+\lvert\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\rvert^{q-1}).

Notice that in (
5.18
), we have also used the assumption (
3.22
).

Similarly, we show that for every
\delta>0
,

\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\leq C(p,c_{F^{\prime}},\tilde{c}_{F})(1+\lvert\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert^{p-1}).

(5.19)

Now, from (
5.18
) and the Sobolev embedding theorem, we infer that for every
\delta>0
,

\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert_{\Gamma}\leq C(1+\|\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{q-1}),

This, together with (
5.13
) yields that

\|G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma)))}\leq c_{r}.

(5.20)

Next, using (
5.19
) and the embedding of
V_{1}\hookrightarrow L^{\frac{6(p-1)}{5}}(\mathcal{O})
, we deduce that for every
\delta>0
,

\|F^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\|_{L^{6/5}(\mathcal{O})}^{6/5}\leq C+C\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\frac{6(p-1)}{5}}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C(1+\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{V_{1}}).

Then, by the estimate (
5.11
), we infer that there exists
c_{r}>0
independent of
\delta
such that

\|F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\|_{L^{\frac{6r}{5}}(\bar{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathcal{O})))}\leq c_{r}.

(5.21)

Step3:

Since
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{2(p-1)}(\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle\mbox{ if }p\geq 2\;\mbox{ when }\;d=2,

\displaystyle\mbox{ if }2\leq p\leq 4\;\mbox{ when }\;d=3,

we infer from (
5.19
) in
Step2
that for every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\leq C\left(1+\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2(p-1)}(\mathcal{O})}^{p-1}\right)\leq C\left(1+\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{V_{1}}^{p-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mbox{ if }p\geq 2\;\mbox{ when }\;d=2,

(5.22)

\displaystyle\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\leq C\left(1+\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{V_{1}}^{p-1}\right)

\displaystyle\mbox{ if }2\leq p\leq 4\;\mbox{ when }\;d=3.

Moreover, by the Sobolev inequalities, see for instance
[
26
, Theorem 10.1]
, there exists
C=C(\mathcal{O},p)
such that for every
\delta>0
,

\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2(p-1)}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{L^{6}(\mathcal{O})}^{\frac{p+2}{2(p-1)}}\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{\frac{p-4}{2(p-1)}}\quad\mbox{ if }4<p\leq 6\;\mbox{ when }\;d=3.

This, jointly with (
5.19
) yields that for every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\leq C\left(1+\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{L^{6}(\mathcal{O})}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{\frac{p-4}{2}}\right)\quad\mbox{ if }4<p\leq 6\;\mbox{ when }\;d=3.

(5.23)

Let us choose and fix
\epsilon>0
. Without loss of generality, we assume
p\in(4,6)
. Thanks to (
5.23
), the fact that
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{6}(\mathcal{O})
jointly with the Young inequality, we infer that there exists a constant
C=C(\mathcal{O},p,c_{F^{\prime}},\tilde{c}_{F})
independent of
\delta
such that,

\displaystyle\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\leq C\left(1+\epsilon^{\frac{p-4}{2p-4}}+\epsilon^{-\frac{p-4}{6-p}}\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{V_{1}}^{\frac{p+2}{6-p}}\right)+\epsilon\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{H^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\mbox{ if }d=3.

(5.24)

Next, plugging (
5.24
) into the RHS of (
5.17
), choosing
\epsilon=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2c_{\ast}}}
, we deduce in the case
d=3
and when
p\in(4,6)
that there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
K,\,\varepsilon,\,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\,\mathcal{O},\,\Gamma,\,c_{F^{\prime}},\,\tilde{c}_{F},\,c_{G^{\prime}},\,\tilde{c}_{G},\,q
, and
p
such
that for any
\delta>0
,

\frac{1}{2}\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(1+\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}+\|\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2(q-1)}+\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{V_{1}}^{\frac{2(p+2)}{6-p}}\right).

(5.25)

By inserting (
5.22
) into the RHS of (
5.17
), we infer that for every
\delta>0
,

\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\leq C(1+\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}+\|\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2(q-1)}+\|\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\|_{V_{1}}^{2(p-1)}),

(5.26)

where
2\leq p\leq 4
if
d=3
and
p\geq 2
if
d=2
.

Step4:
When
2\leq p\leq 4
if
d=3
and
p\geq 2
if
d=2
, the estimate (
5.15
) is a direct consequence of (
5.11
), (
5.12
), (
5.13
), and (
5.26
).

When
4<p\leq 6
if
d=3
, (
5.15
) is a direct consequence of (
5.11
), (
5.12
), (
5.13
), and (
5.25
). This completes the proof of our claim, i.e. (
5.15
).

From now on, by (
5.22
), (
5.23
) together with (
5.11
), (
5.12
), (
5.13
), and (
5.15
), we deduce that for every
\delta>0
,

\|F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O})))}\leq c_{r}.

(5.27)

Further estimates
Fix
(v,v_{\Gamma})\in\mathbb{V}
such that
\|(v,v_{\Gamma})\|_{\mathbb{V}}\leq 1
. Then from (
4.4
), we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-69.12599pt\hskip 69.12599pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-69.12599pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 69.12599pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-69.12599pt\hskip 69.12599pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-69.12599pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 69.12599pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-57.76653pt\hskip 57.76653pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-57.76653pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 57.76653pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.42862pt\hskip 54.42862pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-54.42862pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 54.42862pt\mathbb{V}}}}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla v\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathcal{O}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})v\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}v_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})v_{\Gamma}\,\mathrm{d}S+(1/K)\int_{\Gamma}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}-\bar{\phi}_{\delta})(\varepsilon v_{\Gamma}-\varepsilon v)\,\mathrm{d}S.

Therefore, for every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathchoice{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\big\langle(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-69.12599pt\hskip 69.12599pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-69.12599pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 69.12599pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 8.6077pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big\langle(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-69.12599pt\hskip 69.12599pt}_{\scriptstyle\hskip-8.6077pt\hskip-69.12599pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 69.12599pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\big\langle(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-57.76653pt\hskip 57.76653pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-57.76653pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 57.76653pt\mathbb{V}}}}{{\hskip 7.60774pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\big\langle(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta}),(v,v_{\Gamma})\big\rangle}$}^{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-54.42862pt\hskip 54.42862pt}_{\scriptscriptstyle\hskip-7.60774pt\hskip-54.42862pt\mathbb{V}^{\prime}\hskip 54.42862pt\mathbb{V}}}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lvert\nabla\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert\lvert\nabla v\rvert+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathcal{O})}\|v\|_{L^{6}(\mathcal{O})}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}\rvert_{\Gamma}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}v_{\Gamma}\rvert_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert_{\Gamma}\lvert v_{\Gamma}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\varepsilon(1/K)\lvert\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}-\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert_{\Gamma}\lvert v_{\Gamma}-v\rvert_{\Gamma}.

Next, since
V_{1}\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\Gamma)
, we infer there exists
C=C(\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},K,\mathcal{O})
such that for every
\delta>0
,

\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\leq C(\lvert\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}-\bar{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{V}}+\|F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta})\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathcal{O})}+\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\rvert_{\Gamma}).

(5.28)

Furthermore, arguing as in the proof of Proposition
A.1
, we have

\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\leq\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{2}+2\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\|(\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{\mathbb{H}}.

Therefore, by the Hölder inequality, we deduce that for every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\theta}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{4}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{4}}\leq C\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\theta}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{4}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{4}}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\theta}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{2}\|(\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta}(s),\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{4}}\bigg]\leq C\bigg(\bar{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\theta}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{r}

\displaystyle+C[\bar{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\theta}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{r}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|(\nabla\bar{\mu}_{\delta}(s),\nabla_{\Gamma}\bar{\theta}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg).

From (
5.11
), (
5.13
), (
5.21
), (
5.28
), we infer there exists
c_{r}>0
independent of
\delta
such that

\|(\bar{\mu}_{\delta},\bar{\theta}_{\delta})\|_{L^{r}(\bar{\Omega};L^{4}(0,T;\mathbb{H}))}\leq c_{r}.

(5.29)

Finally, from (
5.29
) and similar reasoning as in (
5.15
), we find that for every
\delta>0
,

\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}(s),\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}\leq c_{r}.

(5.30)

5.1.
Tightness of the laws of the approximating sequences

It follows from the above result that for every
T>0
, almost surely, the trajectories of the process
\bar{\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}}_{\delta}\coloneq(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})
belong to the space
\mathfrak{X}_{T,2}
, i.e. in

\mathfrak{X}_{T,2}=C([0,T];\mathbb{H})\cap C([0,T];\mathbb{V}_{w})\cap L_{w}^{4}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{2})\cap L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{V}).

Moreover, we are in a position to perform a stochastic compactness argument.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then,

the family of laws of
\{(\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})\}_{\delta>0}
is tight in the space
\mathfrak{X}_{T,2}
.

If we identify the Wiener process
\bar{W}
with a constant sequence
(\bar{W}_{1,\delta})_{\delta>0}
and the Wiener process
\bar{W}_{\Gamma}
with a constant sequence
(\bar{W}_{2,\delta})_{\delta>0}
, then the corresponding family of laws is tight in the space
C([0,T];U_{0})
and in the space
C([0,T];U_{0}^{\Gamma})
, respectively.

Proof.

The proof can be done by arguing exactly as in the proof of Proposition
4.7
.
∎

We have the following auxiliary result.

Proposition 5.2
.

Suppose
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then, there exists a subsequence
(\delta_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, a complete probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
, and on this space,
\mathfrak{X}_{T}
-valued random variables
((\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{k}},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta_{k}}),\tilde{W}_{1,\delta_{k}},\tilde{W}_{2,\delta_{k}})
and
((\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi}),\tilde{W},\tilde{W}_{\Gamma})
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{X}_{T}}(((\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{k}},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta_{k}}),\tilde{W}_{1,\delta_{k}},\tilde{W}_{2,\delta_{k}}))=\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{X}_{T}}(((\bar{\phi}_{\delta_{k}},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta_{k}}),\bar{W}_{1,\delta_{k}},\bar{W}_{2,\delta_{k}})),

(5.31)

\displaystyle((\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{k}},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta_{k}}),\tilde{W}_{1,\delta_{k}},\tilde{W}_{2,\delta_{k}})\to((\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi}),\tilde{W},\tilde{W}_{\Gamma})\mbox{ in }\mathfrak{X}_{T}\mbox{ as }k\to\infty,\;\;\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-a.s.}

(5.32)

Proof.

The proof can be done by arguing exactly as in Proposition
4.8
.
∎

For the sake of brevity, we will denote the above sequences by
((\tilde{\phi}_{\delta},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta}),\tilde{W}_{1,\delta},\tilde{W}_{2,\delta})_{\delta\in(0,1)}
and
((\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}),\bar{W}_{1,\delta},\bar{W}_{2,\delta})_{\delta\in(0,1)}
.

5.2.
Passage to the limit as delta tends to zero

The goal of this section is to let
\delta\to 0
and then derive the martingale solution of the original problem (
1.1a
)-(
1.4a
). Note that the argument of the proof is similar to the one carry in Subsection
4.4
, thus we will omit some details for the sake of brevity.

Fix
r\geq 2
. By the previous estimates, arguing as in Subsection
4.4
, we infer that up to a subsequence still labeled by the same subscript,

\displaystyle(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})\to(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{\ell}(\bar{\Omega};C([0,T];\mathbb{H}))\cap L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}([0,T];\mathbb{V})),\;\;\forall\ell<r,

(5.33)

\displaystyle(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\rightharpoonup}}(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{V})),

\displaystyle(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})\rightharpoonup(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{4}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{2})),

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mu}_{\delta},\tilde{\theta}_{\delta})\rightharpoonup(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\theta})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{V}))\cap L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{4}(0,T;\mathbb{H})),

\displaystyle(\tilde{W}_{1,\delta},\tilde{W}_{2,\delta})\to(\tilde{W},\tilde{W}_{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{\ell}(\tilde{\Omega};C([0,T];U_{0}\times U_{0}^{\Gamma})),\;\;\forall\ell<r,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;V)),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\lvert_{\Gamma}\rightharpoonup\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}\lvert_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))),

\displaystyle(F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}),F_{2}(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta}))\rightharpoonup(F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}),F_{2}(\tilde{\varphi}))

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H})).

Moreover, by the strong convergences of
\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}
and
\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta}
above, we have, modulo the extraction of a subsequence
(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}})_{\delta_{n}\in(0,1)}
and
(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta_{n}})_{\delta_{n}\in(0,1)}
,

(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta_{n}})\to(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})\mbox{-a.e. in }Q_{T}\times\tilde{\Omega}.

(5.34)

Furthermore, we claim that

\displaystyle F_{\delta_{n}}^{\prime}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}})\rightharpoonup F^{\prime}(\tilde{\phi})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))),

(5.35)

\displaystyle G_{\delta_{n}}^{\prime}(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta_{n}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\rightharpoonup}}G^{\prime}(\tilde{\varphi})

\displaystyle\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))).

In fact, from the uniform bound (
5.27
) and the Banach-Alaoglu theorem, we have, up to a subsequence,

F_{\delta_{n}}^{\prime}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}})\rightharpoonup\mathcal{K}\mbox{ in }L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))).

On the other hand, we have
F_{\delta_{n}}^{\prime}=\mathbb{A}_{\delta_{n}}-\tilde{c}_{F}I=\mathbb{A}(J_{\delta_{n}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}}))-\tilde{c}_{F}I
and

\displaystyle\lvert J_{\delta_{n}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}})-\tilde{\phi}\rvert\leq\lvert J_{\delta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}})-\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}}\rvert+\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}}-\tilde{\phi}\rvert

\displaystyle=\delta\lvert\mathbb{A}_{\delta_{n}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}})\rvert+\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}}-\tilde{\phi}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq{\delta_{n}}\lvert\mathbb{A}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}})\rvert+\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}}-\tilde{\phi}\rvert.

Therefore, since
\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}}\to\tilde{\phi}
-a.e. in
Q_{T}\times\tilde{\Omega}
, cf. (
5.34
), we infer
J_{\delta_{n}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}})\to\tilde{\phi}
-a.e. in
Q_{T}\times\tilde{\Omega}
. Hence,
\mathbb{A}(J_{\delta_{n}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}}))\to\mathbb{A}(\tilde{\phi})
a.e. in
\tilde{\Omega}\times Q_{T}
. As a direct consequence,

\mathcal{K}=F^{\prime}(\tilde{\phi})\mbox{ a.e. in }Q_{T}\times\tilde{\Omega}.

This proof the first part of (
5.35
). Similarly, we deduce the second part of (
5.35
).

Note that
J_{\delta_{n}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}})\to\tilde{\phi}
a.e. in
Q_{T}\times\tilde{\Omega}
. Hence, by the weak lower semicontinuity, i.e. the lower semicontinuity of
L^{1}(\mathcal{O})
,
and the Fatou Lemma, we infer that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|F(\tilde{\phi}(t))\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|F_{\delta_{n}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta_{n}}(t))\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})},\;\;t\in(0,T).

Similarly,

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|G(\tilde{\varphi}(t))\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)}\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|G_{\delta_{n}}(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta_{n}}(t))\|_{L^{1}(\Gamma)},\;\;t\in(0,T).

Observe that, under the assumption (
3.23
), the limit object in (
5.35
)
-1)
is, in fact, an element of the space
L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;V_{1}))
, while the limit object in (
5.35
)
-2)
also belongs to
L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;V_{\Gamma}))
.

Let us prove the previous assertion in the case
d=3
, since the proof in case
d=2
is similar.
First, by the Hölder inequality and Assumption (
3.23
), we infer that

\displaystyle\lvert F^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\phi})\nabla\tilde{\phi}\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\lvert F^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\phi})\rvert^{2}\lvert\nabla\tilde{\phi}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 2c_{F^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\left[\lvert\nabla\tilde{\phi}\rvert^{2}+\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{3(p-2)}(\mathcal{O})}^{2(p-2)}\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}\right],

\displaystyle\lvert G^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\varphi})\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\varphi}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}\lvert G^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\varphi})\rvert^{2}\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\varphi}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\leq 2c_{G^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\left[\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\varphi}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+\|\tilde{\varphi}\|_{L^{3(q-2)}(\mathcal{O})}^{2(q-2)}\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\phi}\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}(\Gamma)}^{2}\right].

Next, using the Sobolev embeddings
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{3(p-2)}(\mathcal{O})
and
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{6}(\mathcal{O})
, we deduce that there exists
C=C(c_{F^{\prime\prime}},\mathcal{O},p)
such that

\displaystyle\lvert F^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\phi})\nabla\tilde{\phi}\rvert^{2}\leq C\left[\lvert\nabla\tilde{\phi}\rvert^{2}+\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{V_{1}}^{2(p-2)}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}\right],

(5.36)

which in turn, yields that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\lvert F^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\phi}(s))\nabla\tilde{\phi}(s)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}

(5.37)

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\lvert\nabla\tilde{\phi}(s)\rvert^{r}+\left[\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\tilde{\phi}(s)\|_{V_{1}}^{2r(p-2)}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\tilde{\phi}(s)\|_{H^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This, jointly with (
5.35
) and the properties of the limit object
\tilde{\phi}
gives that

F^{\prime}(\tilde{\phi})\in L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;V_{1})).

(5.38)

Similarly, we can show that there exists
C=C(c_{G^{\prime\prime}},\Gamma,q)
such that

\displaystyle\lvert G^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\varphi})\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\varphi}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}\leq C\left[\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\varphi}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+\|\tilde{\varphi}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2(q-2)}\|\tilde{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\right],

(5.39)

and then from the properties of the process
\tilde{\varphi}
, we deduce that

G^{\prime}(\tilde{\varphi})\in L^{r}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;V_{\Gamma})).

(5.40)

Arguing as in the proof of Proposition
4.10
, we obtain

\int_{0}^{t}\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{\delta}(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\delta}(s))\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}^{\delta}(s)\to\int_{0}^{t}\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}(\tilde{\Upsilon}(s))\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}(s)~\mbox{ in probability in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H}),

(5.41)

where

\tilde{\mathcal{W}}\coloneq(\tilde{W},\tilde{W}^{\Gamma})^{tr},\,\tilde{\mathcal{W}}^{\delta}\coloneq(\tilde{W}_{1,\delta},\tilde{W}_{2,\delta})^{tr},\;\tilde{\Upsilon}\coloneq(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})
,

\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\delta}=(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})
,

\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{\delta}(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\delta})\coloneq\mathrm{diag}(F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}),F_{2}(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta}))
,
and

\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}(\tilde{\Upsilon})\coloneq\mathrm{diag}(F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}),F_{2}(\tilde{\varphi}))
.

Let us now complete the proof of the main result of this paper, i.e. Theorem
3.11
.

6.
Proof of Theorem
3.11

The proof of this theorem is given is three parts as follows.

Proof of Theorem
3.11
.

Considering the previous convergences, using the continuity and boundedness of the maps
M_{\mathcal{O}},\,M_{\Gamma},\,\nu,\,\lambda
and
\Gamma
together with the DCT, we see that, after letting
\delta\to 0^{+}
, the process
(\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}(t),\tilde{\phi}(t),\tilde{\varphi}(t),\tilde{\mu}(t),\tilde{\theta}(t),\tilde{\mathcal{W}}(t))_{t\in[0,T]}
satisfies the weak variational formulations (
3.16
)-(
3.20
) in the probabilistic space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
.
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}=\{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
being the natural filtration.

Moreover, by arguing as in (
5.8
), we infer that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|(\tilde{\phi}(s),\tilde{\varphi}(s))\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}\leq\liminf_{\delta\to\infty}\int_{\tilde{\Omega}}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}(s,\tilde{\omega}),\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta}(s,\tilde{\omega}))\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\omega})<\infty.

Furthermore, by the lemma due to Strauss, see
[
48
,
54
]
, that says:

L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbb{V})\cap C([0,T];\mathbb{H}_{w})\hookrightarrow C([0,T];\mathbb{V}_{w}),

and Proposition E.1 from
[
12
]
, we infer that
(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbb{V})
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. Therefore, we deduce that the process
(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})
satisfies (
3.17
). This completes the proof of the first part of Theorem
3.11
, i.e. there exists a weak martingale solution

(\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}(t),\tilde{\phi}(t),\tilde{\varphi}(t),\tilde{\mu}(t),\tilde{\theta}(t),\tilde{\mathcal{W}}(t))_{t\in[0,T]}

to system (
1.1a
)-(
1.4a
) in the sense of Definition
3.9
. Moreover, the process
(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\theta})
satisfies:
(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\theta})\in L^{4}(0,T;\mathbb{H})
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s., cf. (
5.33
).

The proof of (
3.26
)
-1)
is a direct consequence of (
5.33
) and the Banach-Alaoglu Theorem, while the proof of (
3.26
)
-2)
is a direct consequence of (
5.38
) and (
5.40
).

The fact that the process
(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi},\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\theta})
satisfies the equations (
3.28
) and (
3.29
) in the strong sense, follows from the properties of
(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi},\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\theta})
, the fact that
(F^{\prime}(\tilde{\phi}),G^{\prime}(\tilde{\varphi}))\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H})
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-a.s.}
, and the weak variational formulation (
3.20
).

It remains to show that the process
(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})
enjoys the regularity property stated in (
3.30
), that is,
(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{3}),\;\;\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-a.s.}

Indeed, since
\mathcal{O}
is assumed to be of class
C^{3}
and since the process
(\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\varphi})
satisfies (
3.28
)-(
3.29
), appealing to theory of elliptic problems with bulk-surface coupling, see, for instance,
[
38
, Theorem 3.3]
), we infer there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\|(\tilde{\phi}(t),\tilde{\varphi}(t))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{3}}^{2}\leq C(\|\tilde{\mu}(t)\|_{V_{1}}^{2}+\|F^{\prime}(\tilde{\phi}(t))\|_{V_{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}(t)\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2}+\|G^{\prime}(\tilde{\varphi}(t))\|_{V_{\Gamma}}^{2}),\;\;t\in[0,T],

from which we easily complete the proof of (
3.30
).

Proof of (
3.11
). First, note that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{X}_{T}}(((\tilde{\phi}_{\delta},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})))=\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{X}_{T}}(((\bar{\phi}_{\delta},\bar{\varphi}_{\delta})))
, see (
5.31
), hence so
(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})
satisfies (
5.6
). The next step now is to pass to the limit in the resulting inequality satisfied by
(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta},\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})
.
From the embedding
V_{1}\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\Gamma)
, we infer that for every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq C(\Gamma,\mathcal{O})\|F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,V_{1})}^{2}\leq C(\Gamma,\mathcal{O},C_{1})(1+\lvert\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}).

Next, since
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are Lipschitz, we infer that

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U,L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{2}(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{2}(\tilde{\varphi}(s))\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(U_{\Gamma},L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,\;\;t\in[0,T].

Now, arguing as in the proof of (
5.5
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}\nu(\tilde{\phi})\lvert D\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s
