\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}\nu(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\lvert D\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}\lambda(\tilde{\phi})\lvert\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}\lambda(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\lvert\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\gamma(\tilde{\varphi})\lvert\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\gamma(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})\lvert\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath{$u$}}}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\tilde{\phi})\lvert\nabla\tilde{\mu}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{Q_{t}}M_{\mathcal{O}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\lvert\nabla\tilde{\mu}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}M_{\Gamma}(\tilde{\varphi})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\theta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}M_{\Gamma}(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\theta}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s.

Hereafter, we claim that

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\lvert F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}}F^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\phi})\lvert F_{1}(\tilde{\phi})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s,

(6.1)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Gamma}G^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})\lvert F_{2}(\tilde{\varphi}_{\delta})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{\Gamma}G^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\varphi})\lvert F_{2}(\tilde{\varphi})e_{2,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}s.

We preliminary recall that
J_{\delta}
is the inverse function of
f_{\delta}:\mathbb{R}\ni s\mapsto(I+\delta\mathbb{A})(s)\in\mathbb{R}
, which is differentiable with
f^{\prime}_{\delta}(s)=1+\delta(F^{\prime\prime}(s)+\tilde{c}_{F})>0
. This entails that
\mathbb{A}_{\delta}
is differentiable in
\mathbb{R}
. Notice also that
\mathbb{A}_{\delta}(s)=\mathbb{A}(J_{\delta}(s))
. Then, by differentiation and
(P4)
,

\displaystyle F_{\delta}^{\prime\prime}(s)=\mathbb{A}^{\prime}_{\delta}(s)-\tilde{c}_{F}=J^{\prime}_{\delta}(r)F^{\prime\prime}(J_{\delta}(s))+\tilde{c}_{F}(J^{\prime}_{\delta}(s)-1),\;\;s\in\mathbb{R}.

On the other hand, by (
3.8
) and
(P4)
in conjunction with the fact that
J_{\delta}^{\prime}(s)\in(0,1)
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we infer that for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\lvert F^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}(s)\rvert\leq\lvert J^{\prime}_{\delta}(r)F^{\prime\prime}(J_{\delta}(s))\rvert+\tilde{c}_{F}\lvert J^{\prime}_{\delta}(s)-1\rvert\leq\lvert F^{\prime\prime}(J_{\delta}(s))\rvert+2\tilde{c}_{F}\leq C_{F}F(J_{\delta}(s))+C_{F}+2\tilde{c}_{F}

\displaystyle\leq C_{F}\tilde{F}(J_{\delta}(s))+C_{F}+2\tilde{c}_{F}\leq C_{F}\tilde{F}(s)+C_{F}+2\tilde{c}_{F}=C_{F}F(s)+\frac{C_{F}\tilde{c}_{F}}{2}s^{2}+C_{F}+2\tilde{c}_{F}.

This implies that for every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\lvert F^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\leq\left[C_{F}F(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})+\frac{C_{F}\tilde{c}_{F}}{2}\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}+C_{F}+2\tilde{c}_{F}\right]\lvert F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{1}\left[C_{F}F(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})+\frac{C_{F}\tilde{c}_{F}}{2}\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}+C_{F}+2\tilde{c}_{F}\right]\leq C(\tilde{C}_{1},C_{F},\tilde{c}_{F})\left[1+F(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})+\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\right].

Besides, let
\mu
be the measure on
\bar{\Omega}\times Q_{T}
. By (
3.22
) and (
5.33
), we infer that

\lim_{M\to\infty}\left(\sup_{\delta\in(0,1)}\left\{\int_{\{(t,x,\tilde{\omega})\in Q_{T}\times\tilde{\Omega}:\lvert 1+F(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}(t,x,\tilde{\omega}))+\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}(t,x,\tilde{\omega})\rvert^{2}\rvert>M\}}\lvert 1+F(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})+\lvert\tilde{\phi}_{\delta}\rvert^{2}\rvert\,\mathrm{d}\mu\right\}\right)=0.

Hence,

\lim_{M\to\infty}\left(\sup_{\delta\in(0,1)}\left\{\int_{\{(t,x,\tilde{\omega})\in Q_{T}\times\tilde{\Omega}:\lvert F^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}>M\}}\lvert F^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\right\}\right)=0,

i.e. the sequence
(\lvert F^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2})_{\delta}
is uniformly integrable. Moreover, since

\lvert F^{\prime\prime}_{\delta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\tilde{\phi}_{\delta})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}\to\lvert F^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\phi})\rvert\lvert F_{1}(\tilde{\phi})e_{1,k}\rvert^{2}
a.e. in
Q_{T}\times\tilde{\Omega}

we can apply the Vitali Convergence Theorem and deduce the first part of (
6.1
). Similarly we can prove the second part of (
6.1
).

Therefore, (
3.11
) follows from the previous convergences results and the weak lower semicontinuity of the norm. This completes the proof of Theorem
3.11
.
∎

Appendix A
Auxilliary results

We now state and prove the following result.

Proposition A.1
.

Fix
r\geq 2
and
\delta\in(0,1)
. Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.4
, we have the following result: There exists a constant
C_{3,\delta}>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mu_{\delta,n}(s),\theta_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\leq C_{3,\delta}[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{r}],

(A.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mu_{\delta,n}(s),\theta_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{4}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}\leq C_{3,\delta}[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{r}].

(A.2)

Proof.

Let us choose and fix
r\geq 2
. By (
4.52
) and the estimate (
4.47
) in Proposition
4.5
, we easily derive (
A.1
).

To prove the other part, we recall that, cf. (
4.40
),

\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\leq\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{2}+2\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}(\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}).

This, jointly with the Hölder inequality implies that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{4}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}\leq Ct^{r/4}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|(\mu_{\delta,n}(s),\theta_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{r}]

(A.3)

\displaystyle+C\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|(\mu_{\delta,n}(s),\theta_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{r/2}\left(\int_{0}^{t}(\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C[\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|(\mu_{\delta,n}(s),\theta_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{r}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg[t^{r/4}[\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|(\mu_{\delta,n}(s),\theta_{\delta,n}(s))\|_{\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}^{r}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\bigg[\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{t}(\lvert\nabla\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg].

From the above inequality, (
4.39
), and (
4.47
), we easily conclude the proof of (
A.2
).
∎

The estimates in Propositions
4.5
and
A.1
imply the following result.

Lemma A.2
.

Assume that all the hypotheses of Lemma
4.3
hold. Then, for any
r\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C_{4,\delta}>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}(s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{4}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{r/4}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4,\delta}[1+\|(\phi_{0},\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{r}].

(A.4)

Proof.

By the smoothness of
\mathcal{O}
and the regularity theory for Poissons’s equation with Neumann boundary condition, see
[
52
, Section 5, Proposition 7.7]
), we infer there exists
C=C(K,\varepsilon,\mathcal{O})
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{H^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}\leq C\lvert\mu_{\delta,n}-\varepsilon^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{n}F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert^{2}+C\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{1}}^{2}+C\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma)}^{2}

(A.5)

\displaystyle\leq C\lvert\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+C\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert^{2}+C\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{1}}^{2}+C\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma)}^{2}.

Moreover, since
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow H^{1/2}(\Gamma)
and
H^{1}(\Gamma)\hookrightarrow H^{1/2}(\Gamma)
, we obtain for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\varphi_{\delta,n}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma)}+C\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C(\|\varphi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}+\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{1}}).

Furthermore, as
\lvert F_{\delta}^{\prime}(\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert\leq\tilde{c}_{1,\delta}\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert
, we deduce there exists
C=C(K,\varepsilon,\mathcal{O},\Gamma,\delta)
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{H^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}\leq C(\lvert\mu_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}).

(A.6)

Thus, by applying the regularity theory for elliptic problems on submanifolds, see, e.g.,
[
37
, Section 5, Theorem 1.3]
, using the fact that
H^{1}(\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\Gamma)
, we infer that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{\delta,n}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C(\lvert\theta_{\delta,n}-\varepsilon_{\Gamma}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n})-\varepsilon(\varphi_{\delta,n}-\phi_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}+\|\varphi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{\Gamma}})

(A.7)

\displaystyle\leq C(\lvert\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\lvert\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}+\lvert\varphi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\|\varphi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{\Gamma}})

\displaystyle\leq C(\lvert\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\|\mathcal{S}_{n,\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\Gamma))}\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}+\|\varphi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{\Gamma}}+\|\phi_{\delta,n}\|_{V_{1}})

\displaystyle\leq C(\lvert\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}+\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}+\|(\phi_{\delta,n},\varphi_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{V}}),

where
C
may depend on
K,\,\varepsilon,\,\Gamma
, and
\varepsilon_{\Gamma}
. Moreover, since

\lvert G_{\delta}^{\prime}(\varphi_{\delta,n})\rvert_{\Gamma}\leq\tilde{c}_{2,\delta}\lvert\phi_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}
, we deduce that there exists
C=C(K,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\mathcal{O},\Gamma,\delta)
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|\varphi_{\delta,n}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq C(\lvert\theta_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}+\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2}).

(A.8)

Hence, from (
A.6
) and (
A.8
), we find that there exists
C=C(K,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{\Gamma},\mathcal{O},\Gamma,\delta)
such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}^{2}\leq C(\|(\mu_{\delta,n},\theta_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\|\mbox{\boldmath{$X$}}_{\delta,n}\|_{\mathbb{V}}^{2})

(A.9)

From now on, using (
4.47
), (
A.2
), and (
A.9
), we can easily complete the proof of the lemma.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
4.10

Proof of Proposition
4.10
.

Let us choose and fix
\delta\in(0,1)
. Let

\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{n}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta,n})\coloneq\mathrm{diag}(F_{1,n}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}),F_{2,n}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n})).

In view of (
3.4
) and (
3.5
), we see that the components of
\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{n}
are Lipschitz. Thus, by (
4.61
), we infer
\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{n}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta,n})\to\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta})\coloneq\mathrm{diag}(F_{1}(\bar{\phi}_{\delta}),F_{2}(\bar{\varphi}_{\delta}))
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{H}))

\bar{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s., as
n\to\infty
. Moreover, there exists a positive constant
C=C(C_{1},C_{3})
independent of
n
and
\delta
such that

\|\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{n}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta,n})\|_{\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{H})}^{2}\leq C(\lvert\bar{\phi}_{\delta,n}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\bar{\varphi}_{\delta,n}\rvert_{\Gamma}^{2}).

This, altogether with Propositions
4.4
and
4.5
yields that
\{\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{n}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta,n})\}_{n}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{H})))
. Therefore, by the Vitali-Convergence theorem, we infer that

\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{n}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta,n})\to\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta})\mbox{ in }L^{2}(\bar{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{H}))),\mbox{ as }n\to\infty.

(B.1)

Hence, thanks to (
4.61
) and (
B.1
), we can now apply
[
20
, Lemma 2.1]
to deduce that

\int_{0}^{t}\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}^{n}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta,n}(s))\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathcal{W}}^{n}(s)\to\int_{0}^{t}\mbox{\boldmath{$G$}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\delta}(s))\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathcal{W}}(s)\mbox{ in probability in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H}).

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎
